# The decision log of app

> Why app is built the way it is. Each entry says what was decided, why, what was rejected and where it is described.
>
> **Rule:** do not re-propose what was rejected without new facts. If a decision has to change, ask the author and update this file. An entry is not rewritten: a dated amendment at the end of the entry corrects it, or a new entry replaces it, and the old one stays in place marked "Superseded".
>
> The repository was set up on 2026-10-19 from the template of `spec` v0.1.0 (R21 spec), moved on 2026-10-20 to `spec` v0.2.9 (R01), on 2026-11-02 to v0.6.2 (R02) and on the same day to v0.6.6 (R12), on 2026-11-10 to v0.13.0 (R13), on 2026-11-11 to v0.15.0 (R26) and on the same day to v0.15.1 (R28), on 2026-11-12 to v0.16.3 (R38), and on 2026-11-14 to v0.17.1 (R43). How it comes to differ from the template — by the tools in the devcontainer, the rules in RUN.md — is recorded here as a decision, as R46 canvas-api recorded the differences from its model. Next number — R45.
>
> Here entries are cited as `R03`, in other repositories as `R03 app`. Decisions of `spec` are cited as "R18 spec", MathTrail's decisions as "R37 canvas-api", and in a list as "canvas-api: R37, R38". Each log has its own numbers.
>
> Open questions that concern several repositories are in the registers of `spec`: О- in product 13 spec, П- in privacy 9 spec, Т- in repositories 8 spec. What is still to be decided in the component itself is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 2.

## The `spec` tag

**R01. `app` stands on `spec` v0.2.9: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, the links to `spec` pinned to a tag name it, and what came from the template is as in the template v0.2.9. Since then the documents are checked by `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo`.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.1.0 v0.2.9`, read 2026-10-20; Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`):
  - three questions that this repository passed «в реестр spec» are decided:
    - one account can hold both the owner profile as a learner and the profiles of the owner's children: a parent who is learning Spanish does not create a second sign-in (R26 spec). Whom the own key on such an account is for, R26 spec does not decide — that is still О-4, П-4 and Т-9;
    - both questions about `app`'s build now name it. Т-17 — where it is hosted: the hosting is chosen by T03 infra by what the hosting writes to its logs. Т-19 — who deploys it: `infra`, by the same list of exact versions and digests as the services (repositories 8 spec);
    - the card architecture 3.1 spec writes «доступность интерфейса», and the vocabulary concepts 15 spec gives it a row — accessibility — and tells it apart from the opening of a skill and access to content. Beyond this repository's recommendation — it left concepts 4 spec as it was — the item of concepts 4 spec was renamed too: it was «Доступность», it became «Открытие навыка и доступ к содержанию»;
  - П-17 is a new question that R26 spec opened: how a child on a shared device does not get into the profile of a parent who has the category 18+, AI routes for adults and maybe an own key. The recommendation — the parent's profile behind the same PIN as the section for adults, and an own key on a shared device only after О-4 and Т-9. It is decided by T01 api and T05 of this repository (privacy 2, 9 spec);
  - R26 spec also left open the export and the deletion by profile and of the whole account: `api` details them, and what remains of the consent after the deletion of a child's profile is П-14, extended for such an account (privacy 2, 9 spec). The export is assembled by `app`, and it also erases its own data on the device (architecture 4.5 spec): this is an input of T02 and T08;
  - Т-27 — what checks the signature of a package or a build that the shared release workflow of `.github` puts on it. `infra` deploys `app`'s build only with a verified signature (Т-19), and Т-27 is decided with the first release of `module-kit` (repositories 8 spec). This is an input of T01;
  - the amendment to R11 spec closed Т-7 for the API: API contracts are protobuf and buf. The messages and services of the protocol `app` ↔ `api` are an item of `api`'s detailing (architecture 3.5 spec), and the contract is written by epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec`. What remained open in Т-7 is the module format;
  - the widget domain is a separate registrable domain, as R16 spec said, and Т-17 no longer asks about it (architecture 3.11, 3.12 spec). From stage 4 it is one of the settings of `app`'s deployment;
  - R29 spec — the list of the engine's reason codes is a contract in `spec`, written by epic E3. The adult sees the reason code in `app` (concepts 12 spec);
  - R25 spec — the samples page for the first measurement is not part of `app`: its code starts after the measurement. The page moves to the hosting of `app`'s build once that exists (Т-17);
  - R30 spec — the CI checks are the same for all repositories, and `app` will have them even before code: secret scanning, DCO, CodeQL, Scorecard and the documents check are shared checks from `.github`. The merge is held by all checks except Scorecard, the SonarCloud quality gate included, and merges are made only by a merge commit. T21 `spec` connects them: its session writes the CI files here, with `dependabot.yml` and the exclusion of `spec/**` for SonarCloud (Д7 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`), and it also sets the ruleset of the branch `main`. The language checks with the license check and the signed release are shared workflows of `.github` for code: they appear with the first code of `module-kit`, and come to `app` with its first code (R30 spec; architecture 3.11 spec);
  - R33 spec — an analyser's finding that a fix cannot close stays open, and its reason is recorded; the reasons shared by all repositories are in R33 spec itself. Findings on the previous task are closed by the new rule 12 of RUN.md;
  - R31 spec — a release of `spec` comes out by itself, after green CI on `main`: the next patch of the line, immutable, with a GitHub attestation. A repository still moves to a new tag by a task of its own;
  - R32 spec — the documents are checked by the `docs-check` of the `spec` the submodule stands on: links, the repository's own sections and log, the tables «В» and «Д», and the citations of `spec` by the submodule at its tag. An example of a citation is written in inline code (RULES.md, Citations);
  - the amendment to R12 spec and RULES.md, Hard rules: the rule "only compatible with Apache-2.0" is about what ships. A development or CI tool that ships nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships may also carry another OSI-approved license, or free terms for open source, but only as a named exception: in the license check or in the decision that brings it in. This is an input of T01;
  - R23, R24, R27 and R28 spec — about other components: the success corridor, the key for eval, the randomness generator and the derivation of seeds, the graph types. They do not change `app`'s documents;
  - devcontainer — the base image `devcontainers/base` 3.0.10 by digest; Claude Code is installed without package scripts, its `install.cjs` is run by name, and `claude --version` stops the build on a stub; `git.alwaysSignOff` — a commit from Source Control is signed off by DCO (R22 spec, R30 spec).

— *Why now:* T21 `spec` connects the shared checks from `.github` here (R30 spec), among them the required "Documents / docs-check-repo". The shared `documents.yml` builds the stage `toolchain` from the `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` of its caller and runs `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` in it ([`documents.yml`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/.github/blob/ff2a359/.github/workflows/documents.yml), the release v0.1.0 of `.github`, commit ff2a359, read 2026-10-20). And this recipe exists in `spec` only from v0.2.3 (R32 spec): on v0.1.0 the required check would be red and would hold any merge. v0.2.9 is the latest release of `spec` on 2026-10-20, and immutable (`gh release view -R altnovatrail/spec`); `gh release verify v0.2.9 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it by its attestation, and the tag is on commit 5eafb1a. Moving to the latest release at the time of the task is also what Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` requires.

— *Rejected:*
- to stay on v0.1.0 until the first code: the required documents check from T21 `spec` would hold any merge, T05 would go without R26 spec and П-17, and a link to a section of `spec` that the tag lacks, or a gap in the log, would pass unnoticed (Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`);
- a tag older than v0.2.9, even v0.2.3, where `docs-check-repo` already exists: Д6 requires taking the latest release, and rule 12 of RUN.md came into the template only with v0.2.7, and R33 spec with v0.2.8;
- to move without the devcontainer or to carry over its changes selectively: `app`'s container is `spec`'s container (CLAUDE.md, Environment), and a difference would have to be recorded as a decision, and there is no reason for one;
- examples of citations in guillemets, as before: `docs-check-repo` checks a number in guillemets against the log, and R32 spec rejected not checking them.

— *Consequence:* T09 goes first in table 1 of RUN.md, and T01–T03 depend on it. The devcontainer image was not built here — Д1 in table 4 of RUN.md, until Rebuild Container. The CI files — the calls of the shared workflows, `dependabot.yml`, `.sonarcloud.properties` — are written here by T21 `spec`, not by a task of this repository. — RUN.md, T09, T01, T02, T05–T08; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.1, 2.5, 2.6 and 2.7.

## Framework and build

**R03. `app` is written in React 19 and built by Vite 8. TypeScript 7 checks the types, Biome the format and lint, and Vitest runs the tests in Node and in Chromium. esbuild minifies the CSS, `unplugin-icons` embeds the icons, and the packages are installed by npm, which comes with Node.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T01. The versions, licenses and configuration rules are in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1, and the sources in section 4.
- **The framework — React 19.3.1.** The element `<novatrail-canvas>` gets its data by properties, and React 19 "passes all tests on Custom Elements Everywhere" and assigns as a property what the element's class has. For a consumer on React 19, `canvas` has already recorded how to handle the element: properties go in the order of the JSX, events in lower case, a new element per lesson segment by a new `key`, the entry point before the first render (R42 canvas). canvas-api made the same choice (R47 canvas-api).
- **The bundler — Vite 8.3.5 with `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.2**, without Babel. esbuild 0.28.2 minifies the CSS: `build.cssMinify: 'esbuild'`. Vite 8 installs `lightningcss` 1.33.0 under MPL-2.0 as a required dependency and by default minifies CSS with it. With esbuild it stays in the lock file but processes nothing of what ships: it is a development tool that ships nowhere and puts in no code of its own — the named exception `dev` in `licenses.json`, with its reason, as R14 canvas did (R12 spec, R39 spec).
- **The types — TypeScript 7.0.2**, as in `canvas` and `spec` (R73 spec): `tsc --noEmit` checks, and Vite builds.
- **Format and lint — Biome 2.6.4.** typescript-eslint 8.72.1 requires `typescript` below 6.1, and TypeScript 7 has no API; ESLint 9 went out of support on 2026-08-06. Biome alone does the format and the lint and needs no TypeScript — so `canvas` and canvas-api decided too (R12 canvas, R47 canvas-api).
- **Tests — Vitest 5.0.5.** The logic runs in Node, the screens with the real canvas element in Chromium, by the provider `@vitest/browser-playwright` with `headless: true`. The screens are rendered by Testing Library for React, and the input is driven by `userEvent` from `vitest/browser` — through the Chrome DevTools Protocol, not by faking events. Coverage is `@vitest/coverage-v8`, the report `coverage/lcov.info` for `codecov/patch` (R48 spec). The browser is Chrome Headless Shell from the Playwright image by tag and digest, as R13 canvas: the Google Chrome for Testing build under Google's free terms is a named exception, within the two bounds of the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec: NovaTrail does not distribute it and is built, run and deployed without it (Д16 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). In DOM emulators the canvas element does not work: happy-dom without an adapter returns `null` from `getContext()`, and jsdom without the package `canvas` draws `<canvas>` as a `<div>`.
- **Icons — `unplugin-icons` 24.0.0** with `compiler: 'jsx'` and `jsx: 'react'`, through `@svgr/core` and `@svgr/plugin-jsx` 8.1.0. At build time it embeds only the icons the application takes, from the set's package `@iconify-json/<prefix>` by an exact version; `autoInstall` is off. The application has no Iconify components, which load icons from the Iconify API at run time. The set is chosen by T47 `spec`.
- **npm installs the packages**, from the devcontainer's Node 24.21.0: `licenses-check-repo` reads only npm's lock file (R39 spec), and npm itself is named as an exception in `spec` (the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec). `.npmrc` has `save-exact=true` and `ignore-scripts=true`, as the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` requires.
- **The versions** of third-party packages are the latest released no later than three days before the installation, as in R12 canvas. Section 3.1 names them as of 2026-11-02, and the first task of code installs them by the same rule and builds the lock file by `npm install --before=…`. The packages of `@altnovatrail` do not fall under the pause: `@altnovatrail/spec` has the same version as the submodule's tag and comes out on the day of the tag (R73 spec), and the signature of all of them is checked by `release-verify-npm` (R77 spec).

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T01, all by the recommendations of the plan (RUN.md, T01, «Решено автором»). TypeScript 7, Biome and npm are not a choice: they are set by `spec`, the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` and TypeScript 7 itself. The set of tools is the same as `canvas`'s, and both have one contract with the wrapper and one stage with the browser.

— *Rejected:*
- Preact 11: 11.0.0 came out on 2026-09-30, Custom Elements Everywhere tested only Preact 10, and `@preact/preset-vite` 2.10.6 requires Babel and was tested on Preact 10. Preact 10.29.8 is the previous major version;
- Lit 3.3.3 is a library of components, not an application framework: the state, the routes and the tests of the screens would have to be assembled by hand. Vue, Svelte and Solid — `canvas` has no rules for them, and Custom Elements Everywhere did not test Svelte 5;
- ESLint 10 with typescript-eslint and Prettier — for the reason above;
- happy-dom alone with a stub of the canvas: the order of properties, the events and `exportWork()` would be checked on the stub. happy-dom for the screens and a separate suite in the browser: the browser and the exception are needed anyway, and there are more Vitest projects;
- the provider WebdriverIO — it downloads the browser itself during a run, and `canvas` takes it from the image by digest (R13 canvas); the provider `preview` "relies on simulating events"; `@testing-library/user-event` — for the same reason: it fakes events in JavaScript;
- `lightningcss` as the minifier, on the argument "our CSS goes into what ships" — wider than R14 canvas, which put this case off; CSS without minification;
- `unplugin-icons` with raw SVG — the markup would be inserted as a string; Iconify components at run time — a request from the learner's device to the Iconify API (T47 in RUN.md of `spec`);
- pnpm and yarn: `licenses-check-repo` does not read their lock file;
- building by `tsc` alone, as in `canvas`: `tsc` is enough for a package, and an application needs a bundler — modules, CSS, assets and hashes in file names.

— *Consequence:* with the code come the devcontainer, CI and recipes, as [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1, names them. The devcontainer's difference from the template is recorded as a decision at the same time (repositories 1 spec), and the CI files are written by this repository's session (the 2026-10-21 amendment to R30 spec). The rows «в реестр spec» В4 — to Д17 — and В5 — to T47 `spec` — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.1, 3.1 and 4; RUN.md, T01.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the icon set is Material Symbols, the package `@iconify-json/material-symbols` by an exact version, in `dependencies`, instead of `@iconify-json/<prefix>`: so R78 spec with its 2026-11-02 amendment and the amendment of the same day to R39 spec decided, and they took the way of `unplugin-icons` with `compiler: 'jsx'` and `autoInstall: false` as it is. The version of the set is the same as in the versions of `canvas` and `skill-map` in `app`'s lock file, and a test in Node checks it by the `devDependencies` of the installed packages `canvas` and `skill-map`, and the set's notice — Material Symbols, Google, Apache-2.0, its address and version — goes into the build's `NOTICE`. The stage with the test browser and the Biome extension now come from the template, by the recipe `editDevcontainer` (R79 spec), not as a copy of its own. With the code also come the package `@altnovatrail/contracts` — the code of the contracts, of the same version as the submodule's tag — and the client connect-es (R83 spec). So it is recorded in R12 at T12. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* **the icons are embedded not by `unplugin-icons` but by a script of its own, as in `canvas`: `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils` 3.1.7 writes the module `src/app/icons.ts` — the viewBox and paths of only the icons that `app` takes —, and `ci-lint` fails if the module is not the one the script writes. The component `Icon` draws them, by SVG elements, without inserting markup as a string.** The author's answer of 2026-11-10 in the planning of T16 (RUN.md, T16, «Решено автором»).
- *Why:* `compiler: 'jsx'` of `unplugin-icons` 24.0.0 requires `@svgr/core` and `@svgr/plugin-jsx` 8.1.0 ("npm i -D @svgr/core @svgr/plugin-jsx", [README](https://github.com/unplugin/unplugin-icons/blob/v24.0.0/README.md), read 2026-11-10). Both depend on `@babel/core` ^7.21.3, that one — through `@babel/helper-compilation-targets` — on `browserslist`, and that one on `caniuse-lite` under CC-BY-4.0 (`npm view` 2026-11-10). This license is not in `spec`'s `licenses-allowed`, and the exception by the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec does not hold for it: without the package the build does not build. A script on `@iconify/utils` is a way that R78 spec names on a par with the plugin, and the same as in `canvas` and `skill-map`: the same version of `@iconify/utils` and the rule «только пути `fill="currentColor"`». The set is still in `dependencies`: its paths go into the build (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R39 spec).
- *Rejected:* `unplugin-icons` with `@svgr/core` and an exception for `caniuse-lite` — a row «в реестр spec» on R12 spec, and the task would wait for the answer; `unplugin-icons` with a compiler of its own — the type `CustomCompiler` is in its `dist/types.d.mts`, but the README has no such way.
- The 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec names `unplugin-icons` with `compiler: 'jsx'` for `app`: this is the subject of the row «в реестр spec» В45 (table 3 of RUN.md). — R15; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11:* the 2026-11-10 amendment to R78 spec recorded `app`'s way as what the 2026-11-10 amendment decided here — a script on `@iconify/utils`, as in `canvas` and `skill-map` —, and rejected `unplugin-icons` with `compiler: 'jsx'` because of `caniuse-lite` under CC-BY-4.0: this is the answer to the row «в реестр spec» В45, by its recommendation, and the row left table 3 of RUN.md. So it is recorded in R26 at T32. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14:* Vitest has a third configuration, `walk/vitest.config.ts`: the path of the first slice in Node through `playwright`, on Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, by `just walk` by hand and not in CI (R44). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.7.

## Storage on the device

**R04. `app` keeps learners' data in IndexedDB, a database per profile. A task's work with the contexts of its submissions lives until the end of the attempt, and an unfinished one for 30 days without changes; an essay with feedback by a rubric is an artifact and lives until it is erased. The account owner's sign-out erases everything of the account from the device, and the adult sees what lies on the device on the page "This device".** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T02. The table "what — where — how much — when it is erased — into the export" is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2, and the sources in section 4.
- **The storage is IndexedDB, by code of its own behind a narrow interface, without a library.** Each profile on the device has its own database: the attempts with the writing history and the submissions, the essays, the open views of lessons and the state of the demo. Besides them there are a shared cache of open views by package digest and the device's registry: whose account signed in and which databases of profiles lie there. The sign-in session is kept by Firebase Auth in its own IndexedDB database — `app` sets it up through `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alone —, and the media files by the browser's HTTP cache, where `<img>` puts them (R03 api). `app` puts no data of its own in localStorage and sessionStorage, and the sign-out clears them too: what Firebase Auth itself writes there at a sign-in through Google, its documentation does not say.
- **Terms.** A task's work — the writing history, and for each submission its context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission and the submitted work itself — lives until the end of the attempt. Unfinished work is erased after 30 days without changes, and `app` checks the term at start in all databases of profiles. An essay — a task's work with an answer slot bound by `sheet` — is kept from the start of the attempt, together with what was read and the feedback by a rubric, until the adult erases it or the profile is deleted: neither the end of the attempt nor the term erases it. The open view of a lesson and the state of the demo of its examples — until the lesson is passed, or for 30 days without changes. The cache by package digest — 30 days without access, and a withdrawn version leaves it at once (R63 spec).
- **The account owner's sign-out** erases everything of the account from the device — the databases of all profiles, the cache, the registry and the session. Before the sign-out, `app` names what will be lost: the essays that are not on the server (П-8), and the unfinished work. A sign-in of another account erases what is left of the previous one before it opens anything.
- **The page "This device"** is in the section for adults. For each profile it shows the unfinished work, the essays and the space taken; whether the browser granted persistent storage, and a button to ask for it — `persist()` on the adult's press. It warns that the browser may erase the data by itself, and Safari after seven days without the application being touched, and advises adding the application to the Home Screen. It also has "erase" for each essay, "erase the profile from this device" and "erase everything". At the first sign-in, one line calls the same button `persist()`.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T02, all by the recommendations of the plan (RUN.md, T02, «Решено автором»). The model is R42 canvas-api: a draft on the device in IndexedDB, erased by a solved canvas, a new canvas of the same task, 30 days without changes, the parent's sign-out and the deletion of the profile or the account. A database per profile is deleted by one `deleteDatabase` and leaves no remnants in shared stores. `canvas` requires keeping the context of a submission «пока могут прийти пометки», and anything longer is for `app` to decide (architecture 3.3 canvas): marks come within the attempt, and the application sends the work for the analysis once more (architecture 4.1 spec). П-19 leaves the essay on the device: the server does not keep its text and the words of the feedback by a rubric, and for the learner it is the result of their work, not a draft (concepts 8 spec). Firefox shows the `persist()` prompt to whoever holds the device, and at the first stroke that is a child; Chrome and Safari decide without a prompt (section 4). Safari erases a site's data after seven days without a touch, and the adult has to know this in advance.

— *Rejected:*
- one database per device with the profile's ID in each key: the deletion of a profile would become a selective erasure across all stores, and nobody would notice a forgotten remnant in one of them;
- a library over IndexedDB — `idb` or Dexie: a little less code of our own, but a new dependency in what ships, and `app` needs only a few stores and cursors;
- localStorage: 5 MiB per origin, synchronous, and it does not escape Safari's seven days (section 4);
- keeping solved work with marks for 30 more days, or counting any work as an artifact: the device would hold more writing history — with time and pressure — than the platform needs (privacy 1 spec);
- erasing only the session at the sign-out: learners' data would stay on the device without a sign-in, including on a device given away. For the same reason canvas-api advised signing out of the account on a device that is given away or sold (privacy 7 canvas-api). Asking at the sign-out is the same with one press of "no";
- `persist()` at the first stroke, as spec 4.2 canvas-api: in Firefox a child would see the prompt. Without a page of its own the adult would not know what lies on the device and that the browser may erase it;
- erasing at the sign-out by the header Clear-Site-Data: it clears the storage of the response's origin (section 4), `app` has no server process of its own (architecture 1 spec), and a response of `api` from another origin will not touch `app`'s storage;
- caching a task's content by its ID: the instances of a template have one ID (R60 spec; Д42 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`).

— *Does not decide:* the own key — T05, by the general rules of this entry, and the first application has none (R76 spec); what to do with the state of the demo for another version of the module — T11; the export format — epic E4 in RUN.md of `spec` (R09 spec, R76 spec); where the learner model lives — П-7.

— *Consequence:* T05 and T11 take the rules of section 3.2, and the outcome of T08's detailing takes the page "This device", the sign-out and the deletion. The rows «в реестр spec» В7 — to П-9 —, В8 — to the contract `app` ↔ `api` —, В9 — to П-8 — and В10 — to the word "artifact" — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). The size of the writing history in section 3.2 is an estimate, and the measurement is Д4 in table 4 of RUN.md. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.2, 2.8, 3.1, 3.2 and 4; RUN.md, T02.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* `app` keeps the state of a lesson — the open view, the place and the state of the demo of its examples — by the lesson's ID and the package digest on which the lesson was started, and erases it when the lesson has ended: passed, ended early or "Not checked", not only passed. It is also erased by the withdrawal of the version and by the same lesson that `api` gave on another package. The place and the state of the demo go into the export. So R06 decided at T11; the term of 30 days without changes is as before. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the device's registry also keeps the voices of the device — the voice the adult chose per language, and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux. The account owner's sign-out erases them, with the whole account, and they do not go into the export: they are not the learner's data. So R07 decided at T04. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the database of the owner profile also keeps the own key — the provider, the key itself and its last four characters —, but only if the adult ticked "Keep on this device"; without the tick the key lives in the tab's memory while the owner profile is open. It is erased by the events of this entry — the deletion of a profile, "Erase everything", the sign-out, a sign-in of another account, the deletion of the account —, and also by "Remove key", a replacement and an unticked box. It does not go into the export: it is the owner's secret, not the learner's data. The owner profile on an account with a child's profile opens by the same PIN as the section for adults. So R08 decided at T05. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* `app` takes the media files of the device part from `api` by a `fetch` request, not by a plain `<img>` straight from `api`, as R03 api wrote: it gives them to the canvas as a `Blob` — by the function `media` —, and shows the pictures of a lesson's explanations by a `blob:` address. `canvas` brought in the function `media` after the tag v0.6.2 that `spec/` stands on: it is the 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec on `main` of `spec`. The responses still lie in the browser's HTTP cache: `fetch` goes through it. The device's registry also keeps Firebase's `projectId` and `apiKey` with which the sign-in went: if `/deployment.json` names others, `app` erases the Firebase Auth database and the origin's web storage before the sign-in, and learners' data is erased, as before, by a sign-in of another account. So it is recorded in R09 at T06: CSP and CORS depend on it. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.4 and 3.7.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* besides the databases of profiles, the shared cache and the registry, `app` has the store "device settings" — a small IndexedDB database of its own with the device's language that the adult chose. It is neither the learner's data nor the account's data: the sign-out and a sign-in of another account do not erase it, and it is erased by "Erase everything on this device" and by the browser itself. It does not go into the export, and `app` still does not take localStorage. The registry also keeps the term after which the owner profile closes by itself — the adult sets it on this device —, and it is erased with the sign-out, as the voices are. So R10 and R11 decided at T07. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.6 and 3.8.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* the refusal `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS` — "no access" —, while the device's registry names the account, means that another account signed in, and `app` erases what is left of the previous one before it opens anything — as at a sign-in of another account above. So the comment of `AccountService` in `novatrail.api.v1` requires (R98 spec), by option (a) of the row В30 `api`, for `app` too (Д89 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`; architecture 4.5 spec; privacy 1 spec). Without this, `app` would not erase on the device the data of an account that is gone, at a sign-in of another. So it is recorded in R13 at T13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-10:* `app` erases the work of a correct submission not with the end of the attempt, but when the correct answer words have come or their request has ended: "correct" closes the attempt, and the request `GetCorrectAnswerWords` carries this work, because the server does not keep the crop (R97 spec; Д100 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The response to a repeat of this request carries no words — "a response lost is words lost" —, so the work is not needed any longer. So it is recorded at T14 by R13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.3.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* **the page "This device" is built — `#/adults/device`, from the first page of the section for adults (R29).** The executor decided in the planning of T38: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 the tasks T36–T48 ran in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T38, «Решено при планировании»).
- **For each profile of the account** — the owner first: how many unfinished tasks lie on this device and how much space the profile's data takes — "about", by `app`'s estimate from the records of its database: the browser does not name the space of one database, and `navigator.storage.estimate()` measures the whole origin. A profile without a database on the device — "Nothing of this profile is kept", a database that cannot be read — "can't be read", and it can be erased even then; the number of tasks is counted without reading the records and is seen even when the space cannot be counted. A database whose erasure waits for the browser — "Not all of it was erased", with a button to erase again.
- **The warning of loss** (R113 spec) — for each profile while `api` keeps no copies of its work: for now, for all of them, whatever `work_storage` says, because `api` writes the consent but keeps no copies (Д12 `api`). The condition per profile is set by T39.
- **The whole device:** the `estimate()` estimate — taken and how much the browser gives, with the caveat that it is an estimate for the whole site; whether the browser granted persistent storage — `persisted()` — and the button `persist()` while it has not, and the page does not ask a browser that has nothing to say about it; the warning that the browser may erase the data by itself, and Safari after seven days without a touch, and the advice about the Home Screen. The warning about the seven days stands under persistent storage too: whether `persist()` saves a Safari tab from the seven days, the primary source does not say (section 4).
- **"Erase the profile's data on this device"** — the profile's database, through the registry, as any erasure: what the browser did not erase, the next start erases. On the server this changes nothing.
- **"Erase everything on this device"** — the owner's sign-out with everything it erases, and beyond that the device settings and the heartbeat database of `@firebase/app`: "everything" is the sign-in session too. The heartbeat database holds a connection of the SDK itself, and its deletion is `blocked` while the tab is open; IndexedDB does not cancel it (R16), and `app` says "not erased" only of what failed. If the settings were not erased — "Not all of it was erased", and a start does not repeat it: the registry that would remember the erasure is gone.
- Both erasures ask first (WCAG 3.3.4; section 3.9). The page does not repeat the device's language — it is on the first page of the section (R29). The places for the voices of the device (T45), the own key (T46) and the essays (T47) are marked in the code, and the term after which the owner profile closes is E4.

— *Why:* so this entry and section 3.2 describe the page, and "Erase everything" without a sign-out would leave the sign-in session, and a sign-in without a registry, though the button promises everything. The warning is per profile, not one per page, because storage on the server is turned on per profile (R113 spec).

— *Rejected:* "Erase everything" without a sign-out — the argument above; the profile's space from `estimate()` — it measures the whole origin, with the cache and the SDK's databases; the warning about the seven days only in Safari, by the browser's user-agent string — the user-agent string is unreliable, and an application on the Home Screen does not change it; hiding the warning under persistent storage — the argument above. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T38.

## Solving steps

**R05. `app` leads the solving steps on the device, without the key and without AI: the exit condition enables the button "Step done", and "Step done" only leads to the next step — in format v0 the work is read on "Check". The learner ticks the chips "givens and unknown" by a tap, returns to a step already passed, and may always ask for a hint. The default mode is "by steps": the learner switches it on the task screen, and the adult sets the default.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T03. The design and the path by steps of the three modules of the first slice are in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3.
- **The steps** `app` takes from the open view of the solving steps of the attempt's package — it lies in the cache by package digest (R62 spec; section 3.2) — and leads them in order. All tasks of a module go through the same steps, and in a module without steps the tasks are solved without them ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18). The canvas is open on every step: a step does not lock the work.
- **The end of a step.** Each step but the last has the button "Step done", and the exit condition enables it: `none` — at once; `givens_ticked` — when all chips of the task are ticked; `answer_slots_filled` — when each answer slot has something in it: for `choice` the chosen option, for other slots the canvas's `filled`, which `app` rereads on each `historychange` (the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R36 spec). The step does not end by itself: for a slot `anywhere` or `sheet` the condition is met already with the first stroke on the sheet (R42 canvas). There are no timers and no automatic advance (product 7 spec). A step with `givens_ticked` in a task without chips passes by itself, as the format requires. The last step ends on "Check": it has no next step, and its exit condition locks nothing — `app` shows its line, and `answer_slots_filled` enables "Check". In the format examples the last step is `none`.
- **"Step done" does not read the work:** it leads to the next step on the device, without a call to AI, and the work is read on "Check". R69 spec allows reading on "Step done" too, but in format v0 a reading has nothing to give: it has no check by steps (R56 spec), `module-kit` gives the verdict by the observation as a whole, and an early verdict «ждёт вида проверки по шагам в новой версии формата» (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R54 spec). Reading on "Step done" will come with that kind.
- **The chips** — the open fields of the task `givens` and `unknown` — are shown and ticked by `app` (architecture 3.9 canvas): in the tutor's panel, `givens` as a list, `unknown` apart. A tap ticks a chip, a second tap unticks it. There is no wrong tick: the chips retell the task statement, and they need no key.
- **Return** — to any step already passed, by a tap on the strip of steps; forward — only "Step done". A return resets nothing: the ticks, the technique received and the work stay, and the technique is not asked of `api` again — only the hint asks for it, below.
- **The words on a step** are the module's texts and `app`'s own strings, without AI: the step's title, the chips and the techniques, and for the exit condition a line of the interface on what will enable the button, without grammatical gender (R70 spec). The technique is closed, and `app` does not know whether the task has one. When a step with `gives` starts, `app` names to `api` the attempt and the step's place, and `api` answers with the IDs of the task's techniques — empty if there are none — and records what the learner saw (R57 spec). The names and descriptions of the techniques `app` takes from the open technique catalog in the cache (R62 spec). The words of AI come only after a submission: the question of a rung of the hint ladder (architecture 4.1 spec) and what `api` decides for a request after a wrong answer (О-21).
- **A hint on request** — always, in both modes, from the start of the task. It sends no work (R69 spec), and the request always goes to `api`: before the first submission `app` shows the chips from the open view and the techniques that `api` returned, and `api` records that the learner saw them ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19). What the request gives after a wrong answer, `api` decides (О-21).
- **"Check"** — in both modes and on any step. It is enabled when each answer slot has something in it — the same event as `answer_slots_filled`: an empty sheet and empty boxes do not go to the AI. A tap on an option of `choice` only chooses, and "Check" sends. On a pause, after 6 s without a stroke, "Check" blinks if it is enabled, in both modes (R69 spec). "Step done" does not blink on a pause: at the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` it is enabled from the first stroke, and the blinking would call onward before the answer.
- **The mode.** The adult sets the default mode in the section for adults, and for a new profile it is "by steps". It is in the learner's profile on the server, as part of the presentation style, and follows the learner to any device: every task starts with it. The learner switches the mode on the task screen at any moment (product 4 spec); the learner's choice holds until the end of the attempt and is kept with its state of the steps, and it does not change the default mode. In the mode "self" no steps are seen, the technique comes only on request, and the tutor answers only when called ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18). A module without steps has no switch.
- **The state of the steps** — the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips and the IDs of the techniques received — `app` keeps with the attempt in the profile's database and erases together with it (R04). After a restart the learner goes on in the same mode and from the same step. Each task of a lesson starts from the first step.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T03, all by the recommendations of the plan (RUN.md, T03, «Решено автором»). The threshold of the pause, the step start in `api` only for a step with `gives`, keeping the IDs of the techniques with the attempt, the words on a step without AI and the condition of the last step are the executor's proposals, which the plan named, and the author accepted the plan.
- The format's examples of steps put a self-check after "solve" and before the submission: in olympiad maths `Solve it` → `Check the answer`, in school maths `Fill in the boxes` → `Check each line` — "with the task's technique to check it by", and in essays `Read it again` is the last step (section 3.3). If "Step done" on a step with `answer_slots_filled` sent the work, the mode "by steps" would itself give the verdict before the step of the check. As it is, the steps lead to the self-check before the submission, and "Check" before the last step is the choice of a learner who skips the steps, as in the mode "self".
- R69 spec rejected a reading without a verdict for a hint request, and `tutor` has no capability "words on a step" — no prompt, no eval, no form check (concepts 10 spec; architecture 3.10 tutor). A reading of unfinished work gave a verdict before the answer in 94 calls of 196 (R69 spec).
- The steps are a frame, not a check: they have no key, and returning to the plan after "not yet" is an ordinary move in solving. A tick and a return cost only a tap.
- Before the first submission the hint gives what the steps give, without AI (R57 spec), and after a wrong answer what `api` decides. Closing it in the mode "by steps" would leave without help someone stuck on a step.
- "Check" with an empty slot is a call to AI for the sake of "can't read: empty" and a spending of the quota, and `api` will say nothing about the emptiness faster than the device.
- The threshold of the pause of 6 s is the only threshold with a precedent in the project: canvas-api counted a line as finished after 6 s (R16 canvas-api), and the long pause of the measurement log is also from 6 s (R11 tutor).
- Both the adult and the learner choose the mode (product 4 spec), and the learner also learns on other devices. The adult's default holds, and the learner's choice lives in the attempt: so the adult knows what each task will start with, and the learner does not lose their choice in the middle of one. The presentation style is how the platform speaks and shows (concepts 13 spec), and the mode in which the tutor leads or waits is part of it. What the style consists of, `app` and `tutor` decide (О-23).

— *Rejected:*
- "Step done" on a step with `answer_slots_filled` doing the same as "Check": the mode "by steps" would itself give the verdict before the step of the self-check;
- "Step done" with a reading on every step and words of AI without a verdict: a new capability of `tutor` with eval, a call and money on every press, and R69 spec rejected a reading without a verdict;
- an automatic advance by the exit condition: the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` would end with the first line. For the same reason "Step done" does not blink on a pause;
- steps only forward, as in the prototype, or one step back: after "not yet" the learner would not return to the plan and the technique, or would return step by step;
- a hint in the mode "by steps" only after the first submission — the argument above;
- "Check" always enabled — the argument above;
- the mode on the device: on another device it would be different, and the adult would set it on each; the mode only for the adult behind the PIN, as in the prototype — narrower than product 4 spec; a learner's choice that changes the profile's mode: the adult's default would not hold;
- chips that the learner puts in their place in the task statement: the format has no places for chips;
- a "step start" in `api` for every step: the server has nothing to know about steps without `gives` (privacy 1 spec).

— *Does not decide:* what a request after a wrong answer and "Explain differently" give, and how the request and the chips enter the evidence — `api` with `learner-model` (О-21); showing the task's solution — О-7; what comes after feedback by a rubric — `api` (architecture 4.1 spec); the line of the conversation — T08; a widget's state as an exit condition — stage 4 (architecture 3.4 spec); the step "pronounce" — with voice input (concepts 7 spec; О-6).

— *Consequence:* the rows «в реестр spec» В11 — "Step done" in format v0 —, В12 — what the steps and the hint need from the contract `app` ↔ `api` —, В13 — the module's own words on a step — and В14 — the mode in the presentation style — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). Section 3.2 gets a line on the state of the steps, T07 the strings of the interface of the steps, and T08 the screen after the check and the line of the conversation. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.3, 3.2, 3.3 and 4; RUN.md, T03.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the blinking of "Check" on a pause is one per pause: shorter than 5 s and no more often than 3 times a second, and then the button stays as it was. It comes again only after a new stroke or the choice of an option, not after every next 6 s. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` a still highlight replaces the blinking, and the blinking says nothing to a screen reader. So R11 decided at T07: WCAG 2.2.2 requires a way to stop blinking longer than 5 s, and 2.3.1 forbids more often than 3 times a second. The mode "self" is called "On my own" on the button: in Russian «сам» gives away the learner's gender (R10). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3, 3.8 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the default mode is not part of the presentation style but a field of its own in the learner's profile, "default mode", and it does not go to the AI: so `spec` recorded the answer to the row В14 — not by `app`'s recommendation (О-23; privacy 4 spec; concepts 15 spec). `app` names the step start to `api` only for a step whose `gives` gives techniques, and `api` does not record that the learner saw "givens and unknown". A hint on request is by R82 spec: before the first wrong answer — "givens and unknown" from the open view and the technique from `api`, after it — the words of the current rung once more, without AI, and "Explain differently" — the same rung in other words, one AI call per rung, and in the mode without AI there is none. Showing the solution is by R82 spec: to a learner of the category `adult` — from the start of the attempt, to a child — from the fourth wrong answer, and under the adult's setting "ask before showing", on by default, by an adult action behind the PIN; otherwise `api` answers "adult needed". Showing closes the attempt with the outcome "Solution shown", and only then or after a correct answer does the task's solution come to the device (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec). So it is recorded in R12 at T12. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3 and 3.10.

## The lesson player

**R06. `app` runs a lesson on the device by its open view: the segments in the author's order, forward by "Continue", back to the explanations and examples already gone through. The place in the lesson and the demo state of its examples belong to the lesson run on its package — the key is the lesson ID and the package digest —, so the state of another version of the module does not reach the canvas element. `app` derives the mark "sequential" from the skill graph and shows it in "My subjects" and in the section for adults.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T11. The design and the learner's path through a lesson — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4, and the sources — section 4.
- **The lesson** `app` gets from `api` whole, together with the digest of the package on which the lesson was begun: all the tasks of the lesson are attempts on this package (R58 spec; R07 api). The types are `Lesson`, `Segment` and `WorkedExample` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`. A segment and a part of a demo have no ID: their place names them, from 1 ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).
- **The lesson screen.** In the header, on every segment, is the lesson's title, so the learner sees it before each task too, as the format requires, and under it a strip of the segments by their places. `app` draws an explanation itself, without the canvas, and an example with a new canvas element per segment: `workedExample`, then the `demoState` of this run, and the new state comes by the event `demochange` (the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R36 spec). The learner opens the parts of the demo and hides the whole demo with the element's buttons "Next" and "Demo" (R42 canvas). A lesson's task is the task screen of section 3.3 under the lesson's header.
- **"Continue"** is the button of `app` to the next segment; it is not called "Next", so as not to clash with the canvas's button. On an explanation and an example it is enabled at once: the demo does not lock it. On a task it appears when `api` has said that the task has ended — so the format requires. There is no automatic move on (product 7 spec).
- **Back** — to any explanation and example already gone through, by a tap on the strip of segments; an example opens with its demo state, and the lesson's place does not change: "Continue" leads through the explanations and examples gone through back to it, past the segments of ended tasks. The segment of a task that has ended does not open again: the attempt is closed, and to solve the task again is a new attempt; for a task that is not an essay, the work and the open view are erased too (R04). From an unfinished task the learner looks into an example and comes back: the task's work lies in the profile database, and the new element gets it by the property `history`.
- **The lesson state** — the open view, the place and the demo state of each example by its place — `app` keeps in the profile database under the key "lesson ID, package digest". The place is the segment the learner has reached, or a consolidation task after a segment; when `api` has said that the task at the place has ended, the place moves on at once. The end of the run erases the state: the lesson passed, ended early or ended "unchecked" on a withdrawn version and on a version outside the window (R63 spec; R07 api) —, and also the withdrawal of the version and the same lesson that `api` gave on another package. A lesson on another package is a new run, from the first segment and with the demos from the start. A record that the element would reject — `opened` not a whole number from 0 to the number of parts, or `hidden` neither `true` nor `false` — means a damaged database: `app` does not give it, and the demo begins from the start.
- **Opening a lesson.** Which attempts of the lesson have ended and which has not, `api` knows — they are its attempts. The lesson opens on the later of two places: its own, from the lesson state, and the one that comes after the last ended task. On another device, where there is no lesson state, this is the place after the last ended task, and if none has ended, the first segment; the demos from the start, and an unfinished task by the same attempt with a clean sheet: the work lies on the device where it was written (R04).
- **The end of a lesson.** The ordinary end is always "Continue" on the last segment, whatever its kind: `app` tells `api` that the lesson is passed, and erases the lesson state. `api` itself ends a lesson only by an unusual path — early or "unchecked" —, and then `app` does not send "lesson passed". The end screen is the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose, without a score or a comparison (product 7 spec); only the adult sees the reason code (concepts 12 spec). A lesson that ended "unchecked" is a neutral phrase (R04 spec).
- **The engine choice.** `engine` chooses the next lesson or task, and does not reorder the segments (architecture 3.8 spec). A task outside a lesson is the task screen without the lesson's header. Whether the engine adds a consolidation task to the lesson or ends it sooner, `app` learns from the answer of `api` at the end of a task: the skills of the lesson are closed, and whether a verdict assesses them, `app` does not know. A consolidation task is an attempt of the lesson under its header, without a place of its own in the strip of segments.
- **The mark "sequential"** is on a module whose skill graph is a chain: each skill of the module stands in a tier of its own, and there are at least two skills. Anchor skills give no tiers and are not counted; a module without a graph and with one skill has no mark. `app` takes the tiers ready — the `skill_tiers` that `module-kit` computes and `api` gives to the skill map ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 12 and 25): a module is a chain when each tier holds one skill. The mark is seen on the module's card in "My subjects" and on the module's page in the section for adults.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T11, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T11, «Решено автором»). "Continue" on an example without a lock, the button's name, the end of the lesson by the last segment, the end screen, consolidation from the answer of `api`, the check of the record and the count of the chain are the executor's proposals, which the plan named and the author accepted. The plan counted the chain by `prerequisites` with its own code; after the review it is by `skill_tiers`, without a second count of the tiers.
- Back — for the sake of the example: R58 spec rejected a demo over a lesson's task, and to reread an example while stuck on a task is possible only by going back to it. A task, though, is an attempt: to solve an ended task again is a new attempt, evidence and an engine choice, and the lesson names the task once. To open it read-only would mean keeping the task's open view after the end of the attempt, against R04; an essay stays on the device anyway, and where it is reread, T08 decides.
- The place in the lesson moves on as soon as the task has ended, and the lesson opens on the later of its own place and the answer of `api`: otherwise a restart or another device would open a task that has ended and does not open.
- Within one run the version does not change: the lesson holds the digest of its package (R07 api). The state of another version occurs only when the former run has ended — most often "unchecked" on a withdrawn version —, and the lesson is begun anew. The key with the digest tells such runs apart without comparing the content, and the element will not make this comparison: a state that fits by the number of parts it takes silently, even if the parts are different (R42 canvas).
- The place on the device adds no data on the server (privacy 1 spec): `api` knows the attempts of the lesson anyway. The price: on another device the learner loses the place among the explanations and examples after the last ended task and the opened parts of the demo, and that is a few taps.
- The mark speaks of the order of the module's lessons, not of the segments: the segments go in the author's order in any lesson. So its place is where a module is chosen. The graph orders the lessons of different skills wholly exactly when each skill stands in a tier of its own, and an extra link — a skill needs both `a` and `b`, and `b` needs `a` — does not change the order ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 12 and 21; `format/vectors/tiers/chain.yaml`).
- An example is neither a task nor evidence (R58 spec): to lock "Continue" with the demo would mean pressing the learner, and the learner can come back to the example.
- Without "lesson passed" from `app`, `api` would not learn that the learner has read the explanation after the last task to the end, and a lesson without tasks — for example, `reading-model-essays.yaml` among the format examples — would never end.

— *Rejected:*
- only forward, and back to all segments, to the tasks too — the reasons above;
- carrying the demo state over if the example is the same by content, as `sameExample` of `canvas` does — code for a case that does not occur within a run; giving `opened` no greater than the number of parts of the new example — the element will take it silently, and the learner will see open the parts they have not read;
- the place in the lesson on the server: each "Continue" is a request and a new row of the learner's data in privacy 4 spec; the place only on the device: on another device the lesson would begin from the start, and the ended tasks would have to be gone through again or skipped without explanation;
- the mark in the lesson's header too, as in the prototype — the reason above; not showing it in the first slice: the rule of derivation is needed anyway, and the places for it are the same;
- "Continue" on an example, locked until all parts are opened — the reason above;
- one button "Next" for the parts of the demo and the segments, with the canvas's buttons turned off, as «Пересмотреть» of R42 canvas allows: the element makes the demo's buttons accessible and captions them by `lang`, and `app` would have to repeat this and run the parts itself;
- a chain as a strict path, where a skill has at most one prerequisite of the module and at most one skill after it: a graph with an extra link orders the lessons the same way, and would get no mark;
- its own count of the tiers by `prerequisites` of the open view of the graph: a second count beside `module-kit` could diverge from the tiers of the skill map, and the mark would contradict the map.

— *Does not decide:* when a lesson is passed and what `api` records about it, and whether a lesson begun on another device continues the same run — T12 of `api`; when the engine gives a lesson and when a task, consolidation and an early end — the detailing of `engine` (architecture 3.8 spec); how a lesson's task ends by the hint ladder and the showing of the solution — `api` (О-7); the screen after the check and where the learner rereads an essay with its feedback — T08; the words of the buttons and of the mark — T07.

— *Consequence:* section 3.2 gets the row of the lesson state — the key with the digest and the place —, and the end of the run erases it: this is the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04. The rows «в реестр spec» В15 — what the player needs from the contract `app` ↔ `api` — and В16 — what a chain and the mark "sequential" are — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.8, 3.1, 3.2, 3.4 and 4; RUN.md, T11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* "Continue" is a value of `app`'s own, as the third 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec decided by the row В42, and its icon is `material-symbols:arrow-circle-right-outline-rounded`, without a fill, mirrored right to left. It is not in the table of values of architecture 6 spec, and the arrow in a circle looks different from the arrow of the element's "Next", `arrow-forward`, and from the chevron of "Demo", `keyboard-arrow-right`: on the screen of a worked example all three are seen at once. The set 1.2.97 carries it. Rejected: `double-arrow` and `east` — the same bare arrow to the right as that of "Next", only drawn otherwise; `skip-next` — a button of a sound player. So recorded in R13 at T13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.4.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-10:* `app` runs a lesson by `LearningService` (R99 spec), and R06 reads so (Д83 and Д104 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`):
- beside the key of the lesson state "lesson ID, package digest" `app` keeps the run ID that `OpenLesson` gives, and discards the state of another run under the same key;
- the end of the run for any reason — "passed", "unchecked", "not suitable" and "abandoned" — in an answer or in the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` erases the lesson state;
- "lesson passed" — `CompleteLesson` — `app` repeats until `api` answers, and on the refusal `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED` with the places of the segments of tasks that have not ended, the lesson opens on the first one not ended;
- there is no consolidation in a lesson and no early end in the first slice: at the end of a segment `api` does not call the engine (R107 spec). The item "The engine choice" above reads with this: what comes next, `app` learns from the field `next` at the end of an attempt — the next segment, "no more segments" or the engine choice;
- for a task with a rubric, the attempt is closed by the learner's "Done", enabled after at least one submission, and "Continue" appears after it, and feedback without words is `app`'s own phrase "no feedback this time" (R87 spec). A submission of such a task ends with feedback, "unreadable" or "unchecked", not with a verdict, and the adult sees the marks of the criteria in the progress.

So recorded at T14 by R13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2–3.4.

## Voicing

**R07. `app` voices the tutor's words by the device's speech synthesis and always names the voice itself: `localService` is true, and the name has no "Online". On Android and Linux, where the browser does not know whether the system synthesizes on the device, an adult turns voicing on for this device. No voice — no voicing, with a note, and the text always stays on the screen. Whether voicing is on, and its speed, are part of the presentation style in the learner's profile, and the voice is chosen on the device. Voicing is in the first slice, but not in the first app.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T04. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5, and the sources — section 4.
- **The boundary** is R56 tutor: the same text that passed the checks of `api` is voiced, the model writes no separate text for speech, and `tutor` takes no part in voicing by the device. Signs in words, if the synthesis needs them, are written by the code of `app`, after the checks (В63 in table 3 of the RUN.md of `tutor`).
- **What is voiced.** By itself, if voicing is on, the tutor's words from `api`: a rung's question, a hint, feedback by a rubric, a fallback text — and the lines of the panel that come with them: the verdict, the reason "unreadable", the neutral phrase. Voicing begins when the whole text is on the screen (R54 spec). By the speaker button, always when there is a voice: a repeat of the tutor's words and the open texts of the module that the learner reads: the statement, the chips, a step, a technique, a lesson's explanation and the opened parts of an example. The buttons and captions of the interface and the `alt` of pictures are not voiced: that is the screen reader, T07.
- **The language** is each text's own: the module's texts and the tutor's words are the module's explanation language (R46 spec), the strings of `app` the interface language.
- **The voice** `app` names itself, `utterance.voice`, and leaves the choice to the browser not at all. The candidates are the voices whose `localService` is true, whose name has no "Online" and whose `lang` matches the text's language by the basic filtering of RFC 4647: `en-US` matches `en`. First the voice the adult chose on this device, then the voice with `default` if it is the language's only one, then the first. `app` rereads the list on `voiceschanged`.
- **Android and Linux.** There voicing is off until an adult turns it on for this device on the page "This device" (R04), having read why: the device's system chooses the voice, and whether it is a network one, the browser does not know.
- **No voice** — the phrase's language has no candidate, or on Android and Linux the adult has not turned voicing on. The speaker button is disabled, a line beside it says why, and the text stays. The page "This device" names the languages without a voice and how to add a voice in the system — in words, without links (privacy 1, 7 spec).
- **Settings.** Whether voicing is on, and the speed — "Normal" or "Slow" — are part of the presentation style in the learner's profile on the server, as the solving mode is (R05). Both the learner, by a button on the screen, and the adult change it. A new profile of the category `under_13` has voicing on, older ones off. It does not go into the AI call. The voice by language and the adult's permission on Android and Linux lie in the device registry (R04), and the account owner's sign-out erases them with the whole account.
- **One synthesis for the whole app** (R36 spec): a new text cuts off the former one, and the sentences of one text go in a queue one after another; `pause()` is not used; the tap after which a text will come lifts WebKit's ban with an empty utterance before the request; an error is silent.
- **The slice.** Voicing is in the first slice, stage 2, and its code after the first app, before the first child: the first app does not name it (R76 spec).

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T04, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T04, «Решено автором»). What is voiced, the text's language, the order of choosing a voice, the speeds, one synthesis and signs in words are the executor's proposals, which the plan named and the author accepted.
- A remote synthesis gets the text of the phrase from the learner's device, bypassing `api`. Chrome, if the voice is not named, takes any voice by language and does not prefer a local one, and its network voices "Google US English" and «Google русский» send the text to `www.google.com/speech-api`. Edge's voices with "Online" in the name send the text to Microsoft (section 4). The tutor's words are written about the learner's work, and feedback by a rubric quotes the learner's words (R70 spec). Such services have no contract with the platform, and `app` speaks only with `api` and the sign-in service (architecture 3.1 spec). "Output, not data collection" (privacy 6 spec) holds only while the text does not leave the device.
- `localService` is what the browser knows, not the system. WebKit sets `true` for every voice of Apple's system. Chrome on Android builds a voice from the language and the system's engine and does not mark it as a network one, although Android allows the engine to synthesize over the network. On Linux speech-dispatcher has a module of the cloud Edge TTS, and Chrome does not count its voices as network ones. What Google's engine on Android does with the text, Google does not say (section 4). So where the browser cannot know this, the adult decides, having read why, not the browser.
- Devices' voices differ: one has no Russian, another no English, and the `voiceURI` of one voice differs on different devices. Chrome on Android does not choose voices within a language at all. So the voice is on the device, and whether voicing is on is in the profile: the learner learns on other devices too, and the adult sets it once. The presentation style is how the platform speaks and shows: the length of phrases, voicing, the persona, ranks (concepts 13 spec). Voicing is already named in it, and it changes no words and does not go to the model. The default by category — because the presentation style goes by the learner's age, and it can be chosen separately (R41 spec; privacy 3.3 spec). Younger learners read slowly, and for older ones sound unasked would get in the way.
- Without a voice of the needed language the phrase is silent: a foreign voice would read an English text with Russian pronunciation. The note is needed so that the learner and the adult know that the device has no voice, not that voicing is broken.
- The first slice is children from 6, and they read slowly. Voicing by the device costs only code of `app`: no server, no money, no new data besides the setting. In the first app the author, an adult, solves the tasks, and R76 spec keeps the author's first code narrow.

— *Rejected:*
- a remote voice with the adult's consent — the reason above: the text goes to a third-party service bypassing `api`, and Т-13 closes exactly this;
- a voice of another language if the needed one is missing — the reason above; silently, without a note — the learner and the adult would not know why;
- only `localService`, as in the recommendation of Т-13 — on Android and Linux the text could go to the network without the adult knowing. No voicing on Android and Linux at all — tablets on Android would be left without a voice, although the adult can accept this themselves;
- the setting wholly on the device — on each device it would be set anew, and the adult would not know what the learner hears on another. The voice in the profile — a `voiceURI` from another device will not be found;
- the adult's default apart from the learner's choice, as for the solving mode of R05: the solving mode holds what a task begins with, while voicing does not change the content, and there is nothing to guard the default from;
- voicing at stage 3, together with the voice (Т-13) — in the first slice only the adult beside would read the text to the younger learners. Voicing already in the first app — it would widen the first code, and the author does not need it;
- a text for speech from the model — R56 tutor rejected it: a number written in words is not found by the search for the key;
- a button "listen" — concepts 8 spec holds this word for an action of the text canvas.

— *Does not decide:* the words of the buttons and lines of voicing, and the screen reader — T07; the persona's voice — tone and pitch — with the persona (О-13); "listen" of the text canvas — with it, by these same rules: `canvas` has no event `speak` yet (the 2026-10-25 amendment to R07 canvas); voicing by an AI provider and voice input — stage 3 (R56 tutor; О-6, П-5).

— *Consequence:* section 3.2 gets a row on the device's voices, and the page "This device" a section "Voicing"; the device registry holds the voices — this is the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04. The rows «в реестр spec» В17 — why only local voices and what `localService` knows, to Т-13 —, В18 — voicing in the presentation style, to О-23, privacy 4 spec and the epic E3 — and В19 — voicing in the first slice, to product 10 spec — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). What is derived from the browsers' code is checked on devices by the first task of the voicing code — Д5, and it also writes the table of signs in words — Д6 (table 4 of RUN.md). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.4, 3.2, 3.5 and 4; RUN.md, T04.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the reasoning of R07 is recorded in Т-13, and whether voicing on Android and Linux with the adult's permission is a disclosure is a question for the lawyer, П-22. Voicing is in "In" of product 10 spec, and with its speed in the presentation style (О-23); the solving mode no longer stands in the presentation style (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05). That an Android voice may require the network is said by `Voice.isNetworkConnectionRequired()`, and the cloud Edge TTS on Linux by the README of `edge-tts`: `spec` read these sources for architecture 8 spec. The phrase of the Web Speech API "there is no guarantee that any of these implications are true" speaks of latency, price and quality, not of the truth of `localService`, and section 3.5 no longer rests on it. So recorded in R12 at T12. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.5 and 4.

## Own key

**R08. Only the owner profile of the category `adult` has an own key, and `app` sends it only with the requests of this profile that call the AI. On the device the key lies only if the adult so decided on this device — in the owner profile's database, — and otherwise it lives in the tab's memory while the owner profile is open. There is no encryption in the browser: `app` says plainly that the key is not protected from whoever holds the device, and advises a limit and an expiry at the provider. The owner profile on an account with a child's profile opens with the same PIN as the section for adults, at each opening. The key is not part of the export.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T05. The design and the key's path, step by step — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, and the sources — section 4.
- **Whose key.** Only the owner profile's as a learner, of the category `adult`. One account holds both the owner profile and the profiles of the owner's children (R26 spec), and `api` accepts a key only with a request of a profile `adult` and checks this itself (R04 api; Т-9; О-4). An account without an owner profile as a learner connects no own key: the key is needed by whoever learns themselves. There is one key, at one provider.
- **The provider** the adult chooses themselves — from those that `api` opens to the own key for this profile by the admission function (Т-20). Now these are OpenAI and Anthropic (R47 spec, R65 spec). `app` does not guess the provider by the look of the key.
- **Connecting** is in the section for adults, on the page "Own AI key". Before the key's field the page says what will happen to it: it goes to the server of NovaTrail with each request of the owner profile that calls the AI, and lives there only in the request's memory (architecture 4.3 spec); the provider links each request with the account of the key's owner (privacy 5.2 spec); the PIN does not protect the key. And it advises a key of a separate OpenAI project with a hard limit, or a key bound to a separate Anthropic workspace with a monthly limit, with a lifetime (R55 tutor). The page works only when the owner profile is chosen. `app` checks the key through `api`, and after that shows only the provider and the last four characters.
- **Where it lies.** By default only in the tab's memory, while the owner profile is open. The mark "Keep on this device" puts it into the owner profile's database (R04). Whether the device is shared, only the adult knows, so the adult decides, as with the permission of voicing on Android and Linux (R07).
- **What protects it.** There is no encryption in the browser. The PIN is a barrier of the interface (R07 canvas-api; R04 api), and the browser's storage does not protect from whoever holds the device (section 4). `app` says just that, and other measures hold the damage: the key goes only with the owner profile, by default it is not on the device, the provider sets the limit and the lifetime, and the key is revoked at the provider.
- **The request.** The key and the provider go with each request of the owner profile that calls the AI: with work to read, with the analysis and the hint, and with "explain differently", which calls the AI without work (R10 api). With a request of a child's profile the key never goes.
- **A refusal of the key** — without a silent substitution. The owner as a learner gets what architecture 4.1 spec requires for an exhausted limit, with a line of the cause, and the section for adults gets what to do (В62 tutor). `app` moves to the platform AI only by the owner's action.
- **The owner profile** (П-17). If the account holds a child's profile, the owner profile opens with the same PIN as the section for adults (R04 api), at each opening. It closes — and the key leaves the memory — by "Switch learner", on a reload, and when the page has been hidden for 5 minutes or longer, by the device's clock. Without children's profiles it opens without a PIN and does not close after 5 minutes of a hidden page.
- **The key is erased by** "Remove key", a replacement — when the new key has passed the check —, the mark taken off and the events of R04: the deletion of the owner profile, "Erase everything", the sign-out, the sign-in of another account, the account's deletion. The other tabs of `app` learn of this through `BroadcastChannel`. The key has no expiry of its own on the device.
- **The export** — without the key and without a mark about it.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T05, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T05, «Решено автором»). Whose key, the choice of the provider from the list of `api`, the text before connecting, the check through `api`, the last four characters, the key with each AI call, the refusal without substitution, the expiry, 5 minutes of a hidden page and the lock in the choice of the learner are the executor's proposals, which the plan named and the author accepted.
- The providers keep a key on the server, not in the browser: OpenAI — "Don’t share it with others or expose it in any client-side code such as browsers or apps", and Anthropic's SDK for the browser — "Any user with access to the browser can potentially inspect, extract, and misuse these credentials" (section 4). Т-9 chose the device so that there would be no own keys on the server (architecture 7 spec). On a shared tablet this means that a key lying on the device can be got by anyone who holds the device. So by default the key does not stay on the device, and where the adult learns alone, the adult puts it into the database themselves.
- Encryption does not change this. A non-extractable key of Web Crypto closes only the export: "any application that shares the same scheme, host, and port have access to the same storage partition", and "Any application or user that has access to the device storage may be able to recover the key material" (section 4). A password from the PIN is 4–6 digits, no more than a million variants. The secret of a passkey — the extension `prf` of WebAuthn — Safari does not give in `get()`, so on an iPad there is none, and the code of a family tablet is often known to the child too. An honest caveat and a limit at the provider hold more than an appearance of encryption.
- A limit at the provider holds the key on all devices, even a leaked key of its own project or workspace, if the key is bound to it: an Anthropic key without a binding chooses the workspace by a header of the request (R55 tutor; section 4).
