Neither OpenAI nor Anthropic has a limit on a single key — only on a project, a workspace or an organization (section 4) —, so the advice is a separate project or workspace.
- A child who got into the owner profile would send the child's work to the provider under the adult's terms. OpenAI requires not processing the data of children under 13 without zero data retention, and a family's key does not have it (privacy 5.3 spec). So the PIN is asked at each opening, and the profile also closes by itself: a child who was given the tablet will not get back into the owner profile without the PIN. Without children's profiles, the PIN and the closing after a hidden page hold nobody. While the page is seen, the profile does not close by itself: an idle timer would close it in the middle of a task, and the adult, handing over the tablet, presses "Switch learner".
- The platform AI is other contracts and the platform's quota (concepts 14 spec). Moving to it behind the owner's back would mean spending the quota and sending the work under other terms.
- The export is learners' data (architecture 4.5 spec; R09 spec), and the key is the owner's secret (privacy 5.2 spec). An export file is kept and forwarded, and a new key the owner issues at the provider themselves.

— *Rejected:*
- the key always on the device, behind the PIN: on a shared tablet it would always lie there, against what the providers advise; storing the key nowhere: on their own laptop the adult would enter it at each opening of their profile;
- encrypting the key with a non-extractable key of Web Crypto, a password from the PIN or the secret of a passkey — the reasons above;
- the key on the server, encrypted: the architecture has no own keys on the server (architecture 7 spec; Т-9);
- the owner profile by a fresh sign-in — R04 api rejected it: on a shared tablet each switch would become a sign-in through Google; the PIN once until a reload — a child who was given the tablet would get back into the owner profile without the PIN; the PIN also without children's profiles — a barrier that holds nobody;
- the key in the export, or a mark about it — the reason above;
- moving to the platform AI on a refusal of the key without the owner's action — the reason above;
- the provider by the look of the key: a mistake would send the key to another provider;
- the key only with work, as В64 tutor writes: "explain differently" calls the AI without work (R10 api);
- an expiry of its own for the key on the device: the provider sets the lifetime — at Anthropic it is chosen when the key is issued (section 4) —, and a second expiry would only force entering the key anew.

— *Does not decide:* the admission table and which providers are open to the own key — Т-20, the epic E3 in the RUN.md of `spec` and `tutor`; the spending limit — it is at the provider (R55 tutor); whether the key's owner may give it to `api` under the provider's terms — the row В23; the own key for minors — П-4; the first app — it has no own key (COORDINATION.md, «Первое приложение»), and how `api` and `tutor` name the path with the key from an environment variable — В64 tutor; the words of the screens — T07; the PIN of the section for adults as a whole, and the choice of the learner — T08.

— *Consequence:* section 3.2 gets a row on the own key, and the page "This device" a row of the key for the owner profile: this is the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04. The rows «в реестр spec» В20 — where the key is on the device, to Т-9 —, В21 — the side of `app` in П-17 —, В22 — what the own key needs from the contract `app` ↔ `api` — and В23 — the providers' terms on a key given to a third-party service — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). That the screen lock hides the page, and how the clock runs on a sleeping tablet, is checked on devices by the first task of the own key's code — Д7 (table 4 of RUN.md). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.5, 3.2, 3.6 and 4; RUN.md, T05, T08.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the time after which a hidden page closes the owner profile on an account with a child's profile is set by the adult on this device, on the page "This device": 5 minutes by default, 15, 30 or 60 minutes, or "do not close". Closing for protection is also a time limit by WCAG 2.2.1: "if a time limit is included in order to address security concerns, it would be considered to have been set by the content", — and 2.2.1 allows turning the limit off or changing it tenfold. The choice lies in the device registry, as the permission of voicing does, and is erased with the sign-out. The closing loses nothing: the work lies in the profile database. The PIN field is `<input type="password" inputmode="numeric">` with pasting and without `autocomplete` hints, and when `api` has locked the entry, the error names how many tries are left and when the entry opens — if `api` says so (R04 api; the row В37 in table 3 of RUN.md). So R11 decided at T07. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.6 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* R84 spec closed Т-9 and П-17 — as R08 and R04 api decided. The providers' terms allow giving the own key to `api`: so the author decided without a lawyer — not by the recommendation of `app` on the row В23 (R84 spec; privacy 10 spec). R84 spec names the closing of the owner profile "after 5 minutes of a hidden page", and the 2026-11-02 amendment to R08 leaves the time to the adult — the row В41 in table 3 of RUN.md. So recorded in R12 at T12. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* "do not close" is not among the times: the time after which a hidden page closes the owner profile is 5 minutes by default, 15, 30 or 60 minutes. So the author decided in the 2026-11-02 amendment to R84 spec, not by the recommendation of the row В41: "do not close" would leave a child on a shared tablet in the owner profile, on the AI routes for adults and on the owner's own key. 2.2.1 is met anyway: 60 minutes is twelve default times, and 2.2.1 allows changing the limit "over a wide range that is at least ten times the length of the default setting". R84 spec now speaks of an account "with a profile of another category" — that is, not `adult`, as section 3.6 does. The answer of `api` to a wrong PIN is `PinCheck`: how many wrong tries are left, and if this one locked the entry, when it opens; to a locked entry, the refusal `CAUSE_PIN_LOCKED` with the time (R98 spec; the row В37). So recorded in R13 at T13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.6 and 3.9.

## Deployment configuration

**R09. `app` reads the deployment configuration at each start from the file `/deployment.json` at the root of its origin. The file lies beside the signed build, but not in it: at the platform the rollout of `infra` writes it from the rollout list, and in self-hosting the operator does, by the same format. The check is strict — an unknown field and a wrong value stop `app` before the first request to the network, and the build has no defaults and no address of the platform. The CSP is in two layers: the lower one in the signed `index.html`, and the hosting's header, assembled by a template from the same build, narrows it to exact addresses. Sign-in is by the popup window of Firebase Auth.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T06. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.7, and the sources — section 4.
- **The settings** are format 1: `format`, `api` and `signIn`, and in `signIn` — `service` with the value `firebase`, `apiKey`, `authDomain`, `projectId` and `appId`. The file has no secrets: the browser of everyone who opened the app gets it, and Firebase's keys "do not need to be treated as secrets" (section 4). The domain of widgets will come with a new format at stage 4 (R16 spec).
- **Where from.** `app` requests `/deployment.json` by an absolute path, first thing at the start, with `cache: "no-cache"`. The response is `application/json`, without a redirect and no more than 16 KiB. The release archive does not contain this file, and CI checks this.
- **The check.** The fields are exactly those the format names: an unknown, extra or missing field is an error with the field's name. `api` is only an origin, `https`, and `http` only on loopback, both for `api` and for `app` itself. The schema `deployment.schema.json` lies in the build: the rollout checks the file by it, and for `app` it is the same source of the rules.
- **What the learner sees.** Without a connection — "No connection" and a button to retry. Without a valid file — a static screen "This app isn't set up" and a line for the operator: what is wrong. `app` does not sign in and sends no other requests. If `app` is opened in a frame of another's page, it shows a static screen and stops.
- **CSP.** The lower layer is a `<meta>` in the `<head>` of the signed `index.html`, right after `<meta charset>`: its own scripts and Google's sign-in script, `object-src`, `base-uri` and `form-action` closed, and the addresses that the build does not know — any `https:`. The hosting's header is assembled by `csp.json` from the same build: in it are the exact `api`, the addresses of Firebase Auth and `authDomain`, and `frame-ancestors 'none'`. Where the hosting sends no headers of its own, only the lower layer acts.
- **Sign-in** is `signInWithPopup`, and `authDomain` is `<project>.firebaseapp.com`. The hosting needs neither `/__/auth/` on the domain of `app` nor Firebase's files beside the build. The price is a window that is sometimes blocked, and Google's script `apis.google.com` in the origin of `app`: on an iPad, in Safari and on mobile browsers the SDK loads it at each start. The device registry remembers the `projectId` and `apiKey` with which the sign-in went: if they are different in the file, `app` erases the session of Firebase Auth before the sign-in, otherwise the former record with the refresh token would stay in its database.
- **One build.** The build has nothing that depends on the deployment, so the platform and self-hosting roll out the same signed bytes, each with its own `deployment.json` and its own header, and the build and the file go out in one release of the hosting. The rollout list pins the build of `app` and the image of `api` together (Т-19; repositories 4 spec), and the file has no versions.
- **Locally**, the development server and `vite preview` serve `/deployment.json` from `deployment.local.json`, which is not in git, and a proxy leads the paths of `api` to a local `api`.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T06, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T06, «Решено автором»). Format 1, the rules of the check, the reading by an absolute path, the schema and the template of the CSP in the build, the media files through `fetch`, the protection from a frame, the failure screens and the local run are the executor's proposals, which the plan named and the author accepted.
- A file beside the build does not touch the signed bytes and works on any static hosting. Vite replaces environment variables at build time, and copies the folder `public/` into the build "as-is" (section 4), so everything the build knows is signed with it. The facts file of R57 tutor is arranged the same way: «файл фактов в образ не вшивают: `api` читает его при старте, и self-hosting берёт ту же сборку со своим файлом».
- The strict check is also as in R57 tutor: there «опечатка в имени факта не должна молча закрыть путь». Here a typo or a default could silently lead the learner's work to the wrong place: a self-hosting copy with the platform's address by default would send learners' data to another operator. The absolute path and the check of the type are needed because the usual fallback of an SPA serves `index.html` with the code 200 for a missing file, and a relative path from a lesson's page would land in it too.
- The signature holds the build's path, not what the browser gets: the browser gets `/deployment.json` from the same hosting as `index.html`, and does not check signatures. So other measures hold the file: the rollout checks it by the schema from the signed build, the rollout list names its digest (the row В25 in table 3 of RUN.md), and the CSP header narrows where the page may go.
- The header is "the preferred mechanism for delivering a policy", and `frame-ancestors` does not work in `<meta>`. The policies of `<meta>` and of the header act together, and the second "can only further restrict" the first (section 4). The lower layer is what the signed build guarantees itself: whatever hosting serves it, no script will run in it but its own and the sign-in script — neither a counter put in by the hosting nor an injected one. The template `csp.json` in the build is needed so that the list of directives is one for `app` and for the rollout.
- Sign-in by redirect requires `authDomain` on the domain of `app`: otherwise Safari on an iPad loses the user, as R55 canvas-api recorded. So the hosting would have to proxy `/__/auth/` or keep Firebase's files beside the build, which Firebase tells to "periodically download and sync" (section 4) — unsigned files that change without a release. The window works on any hosting, and canvas-api signed in with it too (spec 9.1 canvas-api). The SDK loads the script `apis.google.com` both with the window and with the redirect, so it is named plainly, not hidden. It can be done without only by signing in at Google oneself and passing the result to Firebase — this is the row В26.
- The canvas takes pictures by the function `media`, which gives a `Blob`, and shows them by `blob:` addresses (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec on `main` of `spec`; section 4). So the media files go through `fetch`, `img-src` does not name the address of `api`, and CORS of `api` is needed on the paths without a token too — the row В27.

— *Rejected:*
- an answer of `api` at a constant path — the address of `api` would have to be known beforehand, that is, `api` kept on one origin with `app`, imposing this on the hosting of T03 infra;
- an insertion into `index.html` at the rollout — it edits a signed file, and an inline script needs a hash in the CSP;
- `/__/firebase/init.json` of Firebase Hosting — only this hosting has it, only for the SDK "version 8 and earlier", and without the address of `api` (section 4);
- a build for each deployment, `define` and `VITE_*` — it contradicts the card (architecture 3.1 spec; R03);
- a soft check — an unknown field is skipped, and a missing one takes a default: the reason above;
- the CSP only by the header — on a hosting without headers of its own a copy would have no policy at all; only `<meta>` — the address of `api` cannot be named, and `frame-ancestors` does not work in `<meta>`; a `<meta>` that a script inserts after reading the file — "There must not be more than one meta element with any particular state", and the policy acts only after the insertion; a nonce — Vite tells to change it "for each request", and that needs a server (section 4);
- sign-in by redirect — the reasons above.

— *Does not decide:* the build's hosting and the domains — T03 infra (Т-17); whether `api` and `app` have one origin — also `infra`, and `app` works with both; what the rollout checks — `infra` with Т-19; the file's format in `spec` — the row В24; the signal of versions `app` ↔ `api` — the contract of E3, the row В27; sign-in without Google's script — the row В26; App Check — `api`; the words of the screens — T07; the legal texts of a self-hosting copy — T08 and the row В29; the domain of widgets — stage 4.

— *Consequence:* sections 1, 2.6, 3.1, 3.2 and 3.4 get links to section 3.7, section 3.1 gets `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0` and a build plugin of its own, and R04 the 2026-11-02 amendment on media files through `fetch`. The rows «в реестр spec» go to `spec`:
- В24 — the file's format, to Т-19 and Т-17;
- В25 — what the build's hosting must be able to do, to Т-17 and T03 infra;
- В26 — what the device gives to the sign-in service, to architecture 3.12 spec and privacy 4 spec;
- В27 — CORS and the signal of versions in the contract of E3;
- В28 — the canvas's styles and `style-src`, to `canvas`;
- В29 — the operator of a self-hosting copy and the legal texts, to architecture 3.13 spec.

What is derived from the code of the SDK and from the specifications, and not checked on devices, is Д8 and Д9 in table 4 of RUN.md. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.6, 3.1, 3.2, 3.4, 3.7 and 4; RUN.md, T06, T08.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* `app` has no fonts of its own — only the system ones —, and `font-src` in both layers of the CSP is `'none'`. In the built `index.html` — `<html lang="en">` and a `<noscript>` in English and Russian, and the Russian paragraph has `lang="ru"`. The failure screens are in the device's language (R10), the line for whoever deployed the copy is in English, and the screen "isn't set up" does not address the child: instead of "tell a grown-up", an impersonal line that a grown-up's help is needed. So R10 and R11 decided at T07. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.7, 3.8 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the format of `/deployment.json` moves to `spec` with the first deployment, and then the build carries a copy of the schema from the release of `spec` it stands on; until then the format is this one, R09 (R85 spec). The canvas element's styles are set by `adoptedStyleSheets`, and from the CSP of `app` the element needs only `img-src blob:` (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec). `app` offers to reload not at each version of the contract, but when `api` has answered `unimplemented` or sent an open view of a format version that the build does not read: within a major contract package an old build works with a new `api` (R83 spec). The build's hosting, its headers and the rollout with the file are in Т-17 and Т-19, the addresses of the sign-in service in architecture 3.12 spec, CORS in the epic E3 of the RUN.md of `spec`, and the legal texts of a self-hosting copy — П-24. So recorded in R12 at T12. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.7.

## Interface languages

**R10. The interface of `app` is in English and Russian. The learner's language is a field of the learner's profile that the adult sets, and the screens before the choice of a learner and the section for adults are in the device's language. The module's texts and the tutor's words stay in the module's explanation language, each with its own `lang`. Translations are code of its own on `Intl`, without a library, with TypeScript dictionaries, and the strings do not reveal the learner's gender.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07. The design and the glossary — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.8, and the sources — section 4.
- **The languages** are `en` and `ru`, by the recommendation of О-2. New interface languages are stage 5 (product 11 spec). A learner has three languages (concepts 13 spec), and `app` decides only the interface language: the explanation language is set by the module manifest (R46 spec), and there is no target language in the slice (R41 spec).
- **The learner's language** is the field "interface language" in the learner's profile on the server (concepts 13 spec; privacy 4 spec). The adult sets it in the section for adults — for a new profile, by default, the language in which the section is open — and changes it there too; the learner has no switch. No field — the device's language, and `app` does not write it back. A tag that the build does not know — a lookup by truncation, then the device's language, then English; `app` does not overwrite the field, and the section for adults says that the build does not have this language.
- **The device's language** is for the screens before the choice of a learner — the sign-in, the choice of a learner, the start of work, the failure screens (R09) — and for the section for adults. It is the adult's choice on this device, and without a choice the first language of `navigator.languages` that the lookup of RFC 4647, section 3.4, finds — by truncation of the tag —, otherwise English. It is chosen by buttons where each language is named by its own name, on the sign-in screen and in the section for adults. The choice lies in a storage of its own, "the device settings", apart from the registry: the sign-out and the sign-in of another account do not erase it — this is the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04. The account has no language of its own.
- **Two languages on a screen.** The strings of `app` are in the interface language, and `<html lang>` is the same. The module's texts and the tutor's words are in the module's explanation language (R46 spec; concepts 10 spec), and each of their blocks has its own `lang`, and the name of a module, a lesson or a skill inside a phrase `app` inserts as an element with its own `lang`. To the canvas element `app` gives the `lang` of the interface language: the buttons' captions are in it, and the manifest's language on the statement, the lines of the workbook and the parts of an example the element sets itself (R36 spec). A module's card names its explanation language if it is not the interface language. The names of anchor skills are English (R50 spec), with `lang="en"`. Voicing is in the language of each text, as it was (R07).
- **Texts from `api`** — the legal texts and the consent — `app` asks for in the screen's language, naming the language in the request, not by the header `Accept-Language`. No text in it — the device's language, then English, then whatever there is; a text not in the screen's language goes with its own `lang` and a line saying which language it is in.
- **Translations** are code of its own, without a library. A dictionary per language is a TypeScript module: English is the source, with `as const`, Russian `satisfies` its shape, so `tsc` will not let through a key or a placeholder that does not exist. Placeholders are by name; there is no gluing of strings. The forms of a number are the categories of CLDR through `Intl.PluralRules`; `other` is always there, and a test checks the dictionary's categories against the language's categories. Numbers, sizes and dates are `Intl`, sorting is `Intl.Collator`. A code from the server that the dictionary does not know gets a generic string, not an error. A note to the translator is a comment at the key.
- **Rules of strings.** A string does not reveal the learner's gender, as the tutor's words do not (R70 spec): in Russian — without the past tense and short adjectives about the learner. On the learner's screens in Russian the learner is not addressed: on buttons and in the lines of a step — the infinitive, elsewhere — impersonal phrases. In the section for adults — «вы». The mode "self" on the button is "On my own". The pseudonym and the names stand where they are not declined. The AI does not speak of itself in the first person.
- **The words** are the glossary of terms in `en` and `ru` in section 3.8. The code writes the full phrases by these rules, and the author reads them in the pull request.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T07, «Решено автором»). The storage "the device settings", the choice by buttons, the lookup by truncation, the field without a value and an unknown tag, the language in the request to `api`, `lang` on the blocks, the rules of strings, the glossary and the generic string for an unknown code are the executor's proposals, which the plan named and the author accepted.
- The language is in the learner's profile: the learner learns on other devices too, and on a shared tablet each child may have their own. The same goes for voicing (R07). A child does not need a switch: a chance tap would take the child into a language they do not read, and the adult sets the language once.
- The device's language is for the screens where there is no learner yet or where an adult reads: the sign-in screen is seen before the account, and the adult may read in another language than the child. It has a storage of its own, because the device registry belongs to the account and is erased with the sign-out (R04), and the choice on the sign-in screen is made before the sign-in. The lookup is by truncation, not by "best match": in MathTrail the "best match" of CLDR gave Kazakh, Kyrgyz and Belarusian to Russian (R130 standalone; section 4).
- Two languages on one screen were decided by the format: a module has one explanation language, and the format v0 has no translations (R46 spec). `lang` on each block is needed so that the screen reader reads the text with its pronunciation: "Screen readers can load the correct pronunciation rules" (section 4). WCAG 2.2, 3.1.2 requires this too (R11).
- Code of its own — because `Intl` gives everything two languages need: the forms of a number, numbers, dates, sorting, the names of languages. Libraries would bring their own parsing of messages into the build, or their own build plugin: FormatJS after precompiling leaves the parser in the build — "Precompiling skips runtime parsing but does not remove the parser from your bundle", — Lingui without Babel works only by a plugin with native binaries, and its CLI pulls in Babel, and i18next has the forms of a number as suffixes of keys, and `tsc` does not check the keys (section 4). TypeScript modules, not JSON: from JSON TypeScript infers only `string`, so it will not check the placeholders, and JSON has no comments. The table of captions of `canvas` is arranged the same way — in code, with English by default (R36 spec).
- The platform does not know the learner's gender (privacy 4 spec), and in Russian the past tense, a short adjective and «сам» reveal it. Addressing an adult learner as «ты» and a child as «вы» is equally awkward, and the infinitive and an impersonal phrase suit everyone. «Я прочитал» is the masculine gender and a person where the AI reads (П-15).

— *Rejected:*
- FormatJS, Lingui, i18next — the reasons above; Paraglide — it takes its plugins from a CDN address; `messageformat` — it parses messages at run time. MessageFormat 2 is stable since CLDR 47, and `Intl.MessageFormat` is at Stage 1 (section 4): when it comes to the browsers, the dictionaries will move to it;
- JSON dictionaries, as in MathTrail standalone — the reason above;
- the language only on the device — all the children of a shared tablet would have one language, and on another device the learner would see another; a switch for the learner — the reason above; the account's language on the server — the adult and the child of one account read in different languages, and the language in the request is enough for the texts from `api`;
- the device's language in the registry — the sign-out erases it, and the sign-in screen is seen before the account;
- the "best match" of languages — the reason above;
- the captions of the buttons of `canvas` from `app` — R36 spec rejected this: the package has its own table;
- all the strings of the screens now — the words would set before the code of the screens, and the rules and the glossary hold them even then.

— *Does not decide:* the explanation languages of the slice's modules — `modules` and О-2, the row В33; in which languages the legal texts are and which of them is the main one — the lawyer, the row В34; writing right to left — with the first such interface or module language; the full phrases of the screens — the code, by these rules.

— *Consequence:* sections 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5 and 3.7 take the rules of section 3.8, and R04 the 2026-11-02 amendment on the storage "the device settings". The rows «в реестр spec» В32 — a common scheme of translations of UI packages —, В33 — the interface language in the profile and О-2 —, В34 — the language of the legal texts — and В36 — the name of a tutor's mark — go to `spec` (table 3 of RUN.md). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.7, 3.2–3.5, 3.7, 3.8 and 4; RUN.md, T07.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* `spec` recorded the answers to the rows В32–В34 and В36, and R10 reads with them:
- **The languages and the profile's field** — R92 spec, by the recommendation of `app` (В33): `en` and `ru`, the learner's interface language is a field of the profile, the adult sets it, the contract carries any valid tag of BCP 47, and the interface language does not go into the AI call. `app` asks for the texts from `api` by the method `LegalService.GetLegalTexts`, without a token, and names the screen's language by the field `interface_language`, not by the header `Accept-Language` (R130 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). The module's name and explanation language before the first attempt come from the open view of the manifest of the current version (R99 spec);
- **Translations** — the common rules of UI packages of R91 spec, by the recommendation of `app` (В32) with two changes. The words that more than one package shows are not `app`'s glossary but the table of words of architecture 6 spec, and `spec` holds it: `app`'s glossary takes the captions of the canvas's buttons from there, and a new common word goes there as a row «в реестр spec». The names of anchor skills are not translated and stand with `lang="en"`, as it was;
- **The legal texts** are only in English, and the English text is in force, and on a screen in another language `app` says in the screen's language that the text is in English. So the author decided, not by the recommendation of `app` (В34; privacy 7 spec): a text that is accepted has no translations that could diverge. The gist of the consent — who the operator is, what data and why, to whom it goes, how to withdraw — `app` shows briefly and in the screen's language beside the English text (R116 spec). The texts and their gist come from `api`: the body in English, and the gist in the request's language if the manifest of the texts carries it, otherwise in English, and the answer names its language by the field `summary_language`, by which `app` sets its `lang` (R115, R130 spec). The item "Texts from `api`" above — "no text in it — the device's language, then English, then whatever there is" — is replaced by this;
- **The name of a tutor's mark** `app` writes by the kind, the number and the place, as it was, and the contract carries no link of the mark to a phrase of the tutor: the mark belongs to the words of the answer that brought it (R97 spec; В36 — not by the recommendation of `app` in this part).

So recorded in R13 at T13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.8 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11:* a legal text whose version has a translation into the screen's language `app` shows with the translation too: below the line that the English text is in force, and before the English text, with its own `lang`. The translation has no force of its own, and the consent names the version, not the translation (R141 spec; the 2026-11-11 amendment to R130 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). The texts are still in force in English, and `spec` writes the translations — now only for `privacy-policy` of version 2, into Russian. So recorded in R27 at T32. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.8.

## Accessibility

**R11. `app` meets WCAG 2.2 level AA and, beyond it, two AAA criteria: 2.5.5 — targets of 44 × 44 CSS px — and 2.3.3 — motion by `prefers-reduced-motion`. An answer in handwriting, the workbook's lines and the submission of an answer without confirmation do not meet this standard yet, and `app` says so on the page "Accessibility". `app` recommends the standard to the UI packages. It is checked by CI — its own checks, Biome and axe-core — and by a person on the slice's devices.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9, and the sources in section 4.
- **The standard** — WCAG 2.2, W3C Recommendation 12 December 2024, level AA. Beyond it — 2.5.5 Target Size (Enhanced): the targets of `canvas` are already 44 × 44 (R36 spec), and children aged 6–12 hit with a finger worse than adults. And 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions: motion that an action starts is turned off by `prefers-reduced-motion`. WCAG 3 is a Working Draft, and `app` does not look at it until it is a Recommendation.
- **Declared nonconformances:**
  - an answer in handwriting — 2.1.1, 2.5.1, 2.5.7. Handwriting is a way of input, and the function is the input of an answer, so the exception of 2.1.1 does not fit it (section 4). Choosing an option and everything else is done from the keyboard. A second way to answer is row В31;
  - the printed text of the workbook's lines does not grow with the page's zoom beyond fitting to the width — 1.4.4 (О-24; R34 canvas), as long as the sheet of `canvas` does not scale — row В30;
  - the submission of an answer by "Check" — 3.3.4: a test's answer gets a verdict at once and goes into evidence, without confirmation and undo. A wrong answer does not end the attempt, and the learner corrects the work and checks again, and how to count a submission that the learner corrected at once is decided by `learner-model` — row В31. A confirmation before each check would hinder a child.

  The page "Accessibility" is among the legal pages of `app` (architecture 3.13 spec): the standard, these nonconformances and what replaces them. The EAA expects such information "in the general terms and conditions, or equivalent document" (section 4).
- **The rules of `app`** — section 3.9: a visible label on each button, with the icon beside it; one blink per pause — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05; a PIN field with paste and without `autocomplete` hints; the time after which the owner profile closes is set by the adult — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R08; what is irreversible is confirmed; status messages are `role="status"`; a heading and focus for each screen; scrolling is by the page; zoom is not forbidden, orientation is not locked; the theme is light, the fonts are the system ones (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R09).
- **The UI packages** — `canvas` and `skill-map` — `app` recommends the standard to, but cannot bind them: what follows from it for each is row В30 to О-24.
- **CI:** Biome's `a11y` rules by default; the screen tests search by role and name; its own checks in Chromium — targets, the `lang` of parts, forced colors, reduced motion, layout at 320 px; the contrast of the theme's colors — in Node; axe-core 4.14.0 on each screen, except the subtree of the canvas element. axe-core is under MPL-2.0, a named exception `dev` in `licenses.json`, as `lightningcss` is (R12 spec, R39 spec): it does not ship and puts none of its own code into what ships.
- **A person** — on the slice's devices: VoiceOver on iPad and Mac, TalkBack on Android, NVDA on Windows, Switch Control and Voice Control, keyboard only, zoom 200 %, width 320 px, text spacing. This is a debt until the first code of the screens — Д10 in table 4 of RUN.md.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07, all by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, T07, «Решено автором»). The page "Accessibility", the rules of `app` and what CI checks are the executor's proposals, which the plan named, and the author accepted the plan.
- WCAG 2.2 AA is what the new EN 301 549 V4.1.1 equates itself to and what gov.uk tells the public sector to meet. WCAG 2.1 AA, which ADA Title II, EN 301 549 V3.2.1 and the Russian national standard «ГОСТ Р 52872-2019» require, is covered by 2.2 AA, except 4.1.1 Parsing, which in 2.2 is "Obsolete and removed" (section 4). The law names no level for a private site, or binds it through a contract: the EAA — the sale of a service online, with micro-enterprises exempt, ADA Title II — through the contracts of public schools, and the Equality Act — by reasonable adjustments in advance. The beta's markets are not chosen (П-2), so the standard is chosen not by the law but by the learners: children from 6 and adults, on a tablet and a computer (product 3, 10 spec).
- W3C does not advise AAA as a whole: "It is not recommended that Level AAA conformance be required as a general policy for entire sites because it is not possible to satisfy all Level AAA success criteria for some content" (section 4). Two AAA criteria are taken because they are cheap and children need them: `canvas` already meets 44 px, and `app` has almost no motion.
- The nonconformances are named, not hidden. Handwriting is the core of the slice, which checks the reading of handwriting (R41 spec), and a second way to answer is a decision of the format, the contract and `learner-model`, not of `app`. The goal "AA, except what is named" is more honest than "AA" for a screen that does not meet it.
- Closing the owner profile to protect the child is a time limit too: "if a time limit is included in order to address security concerns, it would be considered to have been set by the content" (section 4). 2.2.1 allows turning it off or changing it tenfold, and only the adult knows whether the device is shared — the same argument as for the own key and voicing (R07, R08).
- 3.3.8 itself names paste into the PIN field as a mechanism: "copy and paste to reduce the cognitive burden of re-typing". `app` gives no hint to a password manager: on a shared tablet the manager would fill in the PIN for anyone who knows the device's code, and "the child often knows it too" (R08). The browser can offer to save the password even without a hint, and `app` will not block that — that is a failure of 3.3.8. So the section for adults plainly advises not to save the PIN on a shared tablet.
- axe-core, because without an automated check only a person would catch the ARIA rules and the contrast on a live screen, and it catches, in Deque's words, "on average 57% of WCAG issues". The subtree of the canvas is checked by `canvas` in its own CI, and in `app` a violation of the package would fail others' pull requests. A person is needed all the same: "Tools cannot check all accessibility aspects automatically" (section 4).

— *Rejected:*
- WCAG 2.2 AA without AAA — `canvas` could shrink the buttons to 24 px; WCAG 2.1 AA — without 2.4.11, 2.5.7, 2.5.8 and 3.3.8; WCAG 2.2 AAA as a whole — W3C's argument above;
- handwriting as an exception of 2.1.1 — Understanding 2.1.1 says the opposite; a second way to answer before the first child — the slice would wait for a decision of the format, the contract and `learner-model`; the standard without the task screen — it would bypass the main screen;
- closing the owner profile without the adult's choice — 2.2.1; without closing at all — П-17 (R08);
- the hint `autocomplete="current-password"` on the PIN — the argument above;
- IBM accessibility-checker — under Apache-2.0, but it pulls in puppeteer, chromedriver and telemetry, and takes its rules from a CDN; only its own checks — the argument above;
- a font of its own under OFL-1.1 — that license is not in the list of licenses, and the exception of R12 spec is only for tools that do not ship.

— *Does not decide:* a second way to answer and how to count a corrected submission — row В31; the zoom of the sheet and what else the standard recommends to the packages — row В30; the accessibility laws by market — a lawyer, row В35; a dark theme; a font for children; the words of the page "Accessibility" — code, by the rules of R10, and its place among the legal pages — T08.

— *Consequence:* R05, R08 and R09 get amendments of 2026-11-02, and section 3.1 gets axe-core in `licenses.json` and the Vitest projects. To `spec` go the rows «в реестр spec» В30 — the standard and what follows from it for the packages, to О-24, — В31 — a second way to answer and a submission that the learner corrected at once, — В35 — the accessibility laws by market — and В37 — what the answer of `api` to a wrong PIN says about attempts (table 3 of RUN.md). The manual checks are Д10, and the theme's colors Д11, in table 4 of RUN.md. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.7, 3.1, 3.3, 3.6, 3.7, 3.9 and 4; RUN.md, T07, T08.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* the standard of R11 became the standard of all UI packages — R90 spec, by the recommendation of `app` (В30), — and it binds `canvas` and `skill-map`: what follows from it for each, R90 spec lists as row В30 proposed. The label beside an icon is always visible in every package (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec). What a package does not meet yet, it declares itself, and `app` names it on the page "Accessibility". A second way to answer is О-25 (В31): its recommendation is a keyboard or digit buttons that the adult turns on, — and `learner-model` added to it on 2026-11-07: an answer by the second way is evidence with the weight of handwriting, and a submission whose verdict the learner saw, the model always counts (R06 learner-model). Only a submission cancelled before the verdict may go uncounted, and whether it needs a confirmation before the check is for `app` to decide. R11 already rejected a confirmation before each check: it would hinder a child. So 3.3.4 stays a declared nonconformance, and it goes away with the second way to answer, if that gives a correction of the answer before the verdict. The accessibility laws by market are П-25 (В35), and the author decides legal questions by Claude's analysis (R112 spec). So it is recorded in R13 at T13. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* with canvas 0.3.15 (the third 2026-11-12 amendment to R15) the declared nonconformances of the workbook's lines are different. The executor decided it in the planning of T36: the author was unavailable by a one-time instruction of 2026-11-12, and looks at the decision in the morning (RUN.md, T36, «Решено при планировании»). The list is checked on the element itself, by tests of `app` in Chromium, and not taken from an unmerged branch of `spec` (Д218 in DEBT.md of `spec`, branch `e10-2`):
- **1.4.4 goes away.** The printing of the workbook's lines and the sheet grow up to 200 % by the buttons "Zoom in" and "Zoom out" of the canvas's row, and the enlarged sheet is scrolled sideways by "Left" and "Right", with two fingers and with the arrows — since T94 of `canvas`, since 0.3.13. Test: after "Zoom in" all the way — no more than eight presses — the sheet is twice as wide as the element it was fitted to, and the printing, by the sum of its pieces, too, and the container is the region "Workbook" with "Left" and "Right". The width of one narrow piece, such as "r", does not double to the pixel by its glyph, so the printing is measured by the sum.
- **1.4.12 comes.** The printing of the workbook's lines — numbers, pieces of text and values of a demo — does not take the user's text spacing: a piece's place is set by the page rule of `spec`, `format/page.yaml`, not by the device's font (architecture 3.10 canvas). The statement above the workbook takes it. Instead — larger printing, up to 200 %. Test: under the spacing of 1.4.12 on the page and on the element, the statement takes it, and the widths of the printed pieces stay the same. When it goes away — there is no date, only if the page rule of `spec` gives the lines room for spacing (В77 `canvas`).
- **3.1.2 does not come for `app`.** The canvas declares it for an element without the attribute `lang`: the name of the sheet's region then sounds in the page's language. `app` always sets the attribute, the screen's language, — and now the type holds this: in `CanvasProps`, `lang` is required and of type `Language`, the build's languages, and an element without it or with a language not in the canvas's table of captions does not compile. Test: the region «Лист» and "Sheet" is in the attribute's language, without a `lang` of its own.
- **The canvas's limits, not nonconformances:** in a low window the row of buttons of an element without a height does not stick and goes away with scrolling (T92 `canvas`), so "Up" and "Down" are at hand while it is visible. `app` gives the element no height by the window: the page scrolls with one finger outside the sheet and with one pointer by the scroll bar and the keys, and the row comes back when the page is scrolled up; a height would give the sheet a scroll of its own inside the page's scroll and two Tab stops. To be returned to by the checks on devices, T51.

An answer in handwriting — 2.1.1, 2.5.1 and 2.5.7 — and the submission by "Check" — 3.3.4 — stay as they were. The page "Accessibility" (T40) takes the list from here. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.8 and 3.9; RUN.md, T36.

— *Rejected:* taking the list of Д218 without checking it — the answer of `spec` is still on an unmerged branch, and it names 3.1.2 for `app` too, though `app` does not have it; declaring 3.1.2 just in case — the page would name a nonconformance that does not exist; a height for the element by the window now — the argument above.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-12:* what the first 2026-11-12 amendment took from the canvas itself, `spec` recorded and gave `app` with the tag v0.16.3: the amendment and the second amendment of 2026-11-12 to R90 spec remove 1.4.4 and tell `app`, with canvas 0.3.14 and newer, to take onto the page "Accessibility" only 1.4.12, and "Zoom in", "Zoom out", "Left" and "Right" went into the table of words of architecture 6 spec — the same words as canvas 0.3.15 has. The page and the dictionaries do not change, Д15 is closed (R38). — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.8 and 3.9; RUN.md, T53.

All are by the recommendations of `app` but two: the default mode became a field of its own of the profile (В14) — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05, — and `api` gets the own key without a lawyer's answer (В23) — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R08. В24 is recorded with a refinement about the time limit — R85 spec, — В26 without a new question, and В27 with the refinement of R83 spec about the reload;

The languages and accessibility there still wait for О-24 — the tag does not know rows В30–В40 yet;
  - R78 spec — the icons Material Symbols, R79 spec — the stage with the browser and Biome in the template, R80 spec — the learner model in the database of `api`, R81 spec — `spec` reads the rows «в реестр spec» itself, on the repository's `main`, R82 spec — showing the solution and a hint on request, R83 spec — the contracts in proto3 and buf, over Connect, and the package `@altnovatrail/contracts`, R84 spec — the own key, R85 spec — the format of `deployment.json` with the first deployment;

  - the registers: closed О-7 and О-21 (R82 spec), П-7 (R80 spec), П-17 and Т-9 (R84 spec); new П-22 — whether voicing on Android and Linux is a disclosure, — П-23 — `app` sends the clock offset of the device from UTC with the requests to the platform AI — and П-24 — the legal texts of a self-hosting copy; added to О-14 — the quota in dollars per family day, — О-23, П-8, П-9, Т-13, Т-17 and Т-19;
  - RULES.md: nobody carries a row «в реестр spec» to `spec`: its session reads table 3 on `main` of `app` itself (R81 spec). Rule 2 of the template's RUN.md says the same.
- **What changes here:**

The move introduces no differences from the template, and the image has not been built here yet — Д12 in table 4 of RUN.md;
  - table 3 — without В4–В29, with a note above it on where their answers are; the documents' links to these rows now lead to where `spec` recorded them;
  - the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03, R05, R07, R08 and R09, and [architecture.md](architecture.md) — by what the tag decided: sections 1, 2 and 3.1–3.10 and the sources of section 4.

How `app` checks the version of the icon set against `canvas` and `skill-map`, this move decided, as Д61 of RUN.md of `spec` tells it to: by a test in Node by their `devDependencies` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1). — CLAUDE.md; README.md; RUN.md, T12; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 3.1–3.10 and 4.

## The move to `spec` v0.13.0

**R13. `app` stands on `spec` v0.13.0: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, rows В30–В42 are closed by the answers of `spec`, the devcontainer is the output of the v0.13.0 recipe, and what the tag decided for `app` beyond these rows is laid out across its documents by task T14.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.6.6 v0.13.0`, read 2026-11-10; table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`):

  - the legal part — epic E4 of RUN.md of `spec`: the author decides legal questions by Claude's analysis (R112 spec), artifacts on the server (R113 spec), the texts of a self-hosting copy (R115 spec), the legal basis of processing and consent (R116–R118 spec), the DPIA (R119, R129 spec), the contracts with AI providers and the measures for minors (R121–R123 spec), essays and help to a learner under 18 (R127 spec), the legal texts — the part `legal/` of `spec` (R130, R131 spec);
  - the registers: closed О-24 (R90 spec), П-1, П-2, П-3, П-4, П-6, П-8, П-9, П-11, П-14, П-15, П-19, П-20, П-22, П-23, П-24, Т-19, Т-20 and Т-28; new — О-25, a second way to answer, П-25, accessibility laws, П-26, reporting a problem by the learner themselves, and П-27, who the operator is in the texts;
  - RULES.md: the production images are a static Go binary on distroless (R105 spec). `app` has no image of its own, and this rule does not concern it; the template: in CLAUDE.md — "Node.js, Go, buf and just" (Д86 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`), and for the kind `typescript` — the same additions as before, on a new base image.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.13.0, commit 59fd14f. The tag in CLAUDE.md, RUN.md and the links of README is v0.13.0, and `@altnovatrail/spec` in section 3.1 is 0.13.0. That the repository was at v0.2.9, v0.6.2 and v0.6.6, R01, R02 and R12 still say: that is history;
  - CLAUDE.md names buf among the container's tools (Д86 of RUN.md of `spec`). The rules of RUN.md up to «Как устроен план» match the template: its RUN.md has not changed since v0.6.6. In «Как устроен план» there is a new rule: a task outside an epic, such as this move, goes into table 1 even without an epic, its body is in the section «Задачи вне эпика» at the end of the file, and once done it goes to the archive of the nearest epic. So a move to a tag — by a task of its own, as RULES.md tells, — does not wait for an epic and is not lost between them;
  - `.devcontainer/` is the one that the recipe of `spec` v0.13.0 builds for `app` of the kind `typescript` (`just new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.13.0`, `diff -r` is empty): the base image `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base` is 3.0.12-noble by digest instead of 3.0.10-noble. The move introduces no differences from the template;
  - table 3 — without В30–В42, with a note above it on where their answers are; the documents' links to these rows now lead to where `spec` recorded them;
  - the 2026-11-10 amendments to R04 — "no access" with an account in the device registry (Д89 of RUN.md of `spec`), — to R06 — the icon of "Continue", — to R08 — the time limit without "do not close" and `PinCheck`, — to R10 — the languages, translations, legal texts and the name of the mark, — and to R11 — the standard of R90 spec, О-25 and П-25 (Д87 of RUN.md of `spec`);
  - the rest of what the tag decided for `app` — rows Д81, Д83, Д92, Д100, Д102, Д104, Д106, Д107, Д114, Д115, Д117, Д132, Д135, Д142, Д144, Д147, Д148, Д150, Д154, Д157, Д160 and Д166 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` and the decisions R86–R131 spec — task T14 lays out across [architecture.md](architecture.md) and table 2 of RUN.md.

— *Why now:* row Д87 of RUN.md of `spec` says that `app` moves to the tag with the answers by a task of its own and closes В30–В42, and rows Д86, Д89 and Д81, Д83, Д92, Д100, Д102, Д104, Д106, Д107, Д114, Д115, Д117, Д132, Д135, Д142, Д144, Д147, Д148, Д150, Д154, Д157, Д160 and Д166 wait for the same move (RULES.md, Across repositories). While `app` is on v0.6.6, it gives the adult "do not close", reads format 0.2 and does not know the contract by which the first app is written (Д87, Д107 of RUN.md of `spec`). The release v0.13.0 is the latest (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`), and `gh release verify v0.13.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it by its attestation; the tag is at commit 59fd14f. `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` is green on it even before the edits.

— *Rejected:*
- an intermediate tag — v0.9.x or v0.12.0: Д107 of RUN.md of `spec` requires no older than the line 0.9, Д148 and Д150 — v0.12.0, and v0.13.0 also brings the DPIA, the texts of `legal/` and R124–R131 spec, and the next move would have to be made almost at once;
- the move and everything the tag decided in one task: the decisions R86–R131 spec are more than one check can hold (rule 11 of RUN.md), so laying them out across the documents is T14;
- disputing the answers to В34, В36 and В41: the author decided them in `spec`, and `app` has no new argument.

— *Consequence:* T13 in RUN.md; the 2026-11-10 amendments to R04, R06, R08, R10 and R11; T14 — the rest of what the tag decided for `app`. — CLAUDE.md; README.md; RUN.md, T13 and T14; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.1, 3.2, 3.4, 3.6, 3.8, 3.9 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* a done task outside an epic goes to the archive of the current epic, into its section «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные за его время», as soon as it is done, and its summary sentence goes to the start of the section «Задачи вне эпика» of RUN.md; it leaves RUN.md with the epic (rule 9 of RUN.md by the template of `spec` v0.16.3; R39). — RUN.md, «Как устроен план: эпики» and rule 9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13:* RUN.md has no section «Задачи вне эпика» (R41). A task outside an epic stands in table 1 as before; its body is in the part of the current epic, beside the bodies of its tasks, or at the end of the file when there is no epic. Once done, it goes to the same section of the epic's archive as before, and its summary sentence goes to the list of done tasks of the epic's part, marked «вне эпика». — RUN.md, «Как устроен план: эпики» and rule 9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14:* when there is no current epic, a done task outside an epic goes to the archive of the last done epic, into its section «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные после него», and its summary sentence goes to the end of RUN.md, where its body stood, and leaves with the archive of the next epic: rule 9 of the template of `spec` v0.17.1 (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R19 spec), which R43 took in the words of R41. — RUN.md, rule 9 and «Как устроен план: эпики».

## The app's skeleton

**R14. The skeleton of `app` is by R03, with versions as of 2026-11-10: the lock file is built by `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, and `playwright` is 1.64.1, the version of the template's image. The screens live in the hash of the address, by its own code, without a routing library. `vite preview` and the development server do no SPA fallback. The build is checked by `ci-test`, and `NOTICE` names each package that goes into the build.** The author's answers of 2026-11-10 in the planning of T15; the rest are the plan's proposals, which the author approved (RUN.md, T15, «Решено автором»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.7, and the versions — `npm view` of 2026-11-10, section 4.
- **The versions** of third-party packages are the latest of those released no later than three days before the installation, as R03 tells: `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, and the date applies to the transitive ones too. The lock file has 172 packages, and the latest is `@biomejs/biome` 2.6.6 of 2026-11-06; the dates of all are checked against the registry. Against the table of section 3.1 as of 2026-11-02, these went up: `vite` — 8.3.6, `@vitejs/plugin-react` — 6.2.1, `@biomejs/biome` — 2.6.6, `vitest`, `@vitest/browser-playwright` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` — 5.1.0. The packages of `@altnovatrail` will come in T16 the way `skill-map` installed them (R08 skill-map): first the third-party ones with `--before`, then `@altnovatrail` without a date — their version is set by the submodule's tag (R73, R83 spec), and the signature is checked by `release-verify-npm` (R77 spec), — and the check of the lock file's dates against the registry, where only they may have later dates.
- **`playwright` is 1.64.1**, as the image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble` and revision 1248 of Chrome Headless Shell in the stage `toolchain`: without them Playwright will not find the browser (R79 spec). Dependabot `npm` keeps `playwright` in `ignore`, and it is raised by a move to a tag of `spec` in which the template raised the image. So the devcontainer has no differences from the template.
- **The routes are in the hash of the address**: `/#/<screen>/<part>/…`, each part percent-encoded; parsing and building are `src/app/hash.ts`, without the DOM, and navigation and the current route for React are `src/app/router.ts`, on `hashchange` and `useSyncExternalStore`, and the same hash gives the same route object. Navigating to the route already shown adds no entry to the history. A hash that the build does not know is the home screen. The address holds only the ID of content, a lesson or a task, and never the ID of a profile, an account or an attempt.
- **The development server and `vite preview` are `appType: "mpa"`**: a path that does not exist gets 404, as on a hosting without rules of its own, not `index.html`. So `/deployment.json` without the file is not replaced by the page either (section 3.7).
- **The build in CI.** The shared `typescript.yml` of `.github` v0.1.22 calls `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses`, but not `build`. So `ci-test` depends on `build`, as in `canvas` and `skill-map`: a build that failed fails "TypeScript / test".
- **`NOTICE`** is the notice of `app`, a line `<name> <version> — <license> — <copyright>` for each package of the lock file without the mark `dev` — that is, for everything the bundler may embed, the transitive too: for `react-dom` that is `scheduler`, — and a line `Text of <license>` with its text for each license of these lines, now MIT. `scripts/notice.ts` in `ci-licenses` checks this, both ways: a missing line, another version, an empty copyright, a line of a package the build does not take, a license without its text, a package without a license in the lock file. The icon set and `@bufbuild/protobuf` will go in here in T16.
- **`ci-licenses`** is also `release-verify-npm` already now: on a lock file without packages of `@altnovatrail` the recipe answers "no package of @altnovatrail to verify", and T16 need add nothing to CI.
- **CI** — the shared workflows of `.github` v0.1.22 by SHA `c0234058dc1a85edd7b5d579b90f8fdb98ea826b`; in `ci.yml` — the job "TypeScript" with `id-token: write`, in `codeql.yml` — the language `javascript-typescript` (Д9 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`); Dependabot — the group `shared-workflows`, as architecture 3.3 `.github` tells, and the ecosystem `npm` with the group of `vitest`, `@vitest/*` and `vite`.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-10 in the planning of T15, both by the plan's recommendations.
- The hash works on any hosting without a rule "any path → `index.html`": one build both at the platform and at a self-hosting copy, whose hosting `spec` does not know (R09). The browser does not send the fragment after `#` to the server — neither in the request nor in `Referer`, — so the hosting's logs do not learn which screen the learner is on (privacy 1 spec). The browser's "Back" goes through the screens. The own code is two small functions and a hook: a library would bring into what ships its own code, its own license in `NOTICE` and its own releases.
- `playwright` must match the image, and the template's image is still on 1.64.1: 1.65.0 of 2026-11-05 expects revision 1254, and its Dependabot pull request would be red until the image is raised by hand.
- MIT requires that "the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software", and the build embeds `react`, `react-dom` and `scheduler` whole.

— *Rejected:*
- History API paths with its own code or `wouter`: each hosting needs a redirect rule, a row «в реестр spec» for `infra` and the self-hosting copies, and the screen's path is in the hosting's logs; no addresses at all — "Back" would lead out of the app, and a reload to the start; `react-router` 7.18.5 and `@tanstack/react-router` 1.170.46 — libraries of 4.8 and 1.1 MB for a few screens;
- `playwright` 1.65.0 now — an image of its own and revision 1254 in the Dockerfile, a difference from the template and a row «в реестр spec» (R79 spec);
- `NOTICE` only by `dependencies`: the transitive `scheduler` would go into the build without a notice;
- `build` as a job of its own in `ci.yml`: one more check in the ruleset, and `ci-test` builds before the tests anyway.

— *Consequence:* the table of section 3.1 — the versions of the lock file, section 3.7 — the routes on the hash and `appType`; T16 installs the packages of `@altnovatrail` by the way above and completes `NOTICE`. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.7 and 4; RUN.md, T15.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* `playwright` is 1.65.0, as the image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.65.0-noble` and revision 1254 of Chrome Headless Shell: the template of `spec` raised them by the 2026-11-11 amendment to R79 spec, and `app` took them by the move to v0.16.3 — with no difference of its own from the template, as before (R38). — `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `package.json`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14:* the stage `toolchain` also carries WebKit 2388 and Firefox 1572 from the same image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.65.0-noble`, a `COPY` each, with the libraries of the three engines and the fonts of Playwright's list `tools` from the snapshot `20261021T000000Z`: the template of `spec` v0.17.1 gives them (T141; the 2026-11-13 amendment to R79 spec), and `app` took them by the move (R43), with no difference of its own. They are named exceptions by the 2026-11-13 amendment to R12 spec, as Chrome Headless Shell is, and the tag, the digest and the three revisions move together with `playwright` in `package.json`. The default font of the image becomes WenQuanYi Zen Hei, in Chromium too. — `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.claude/rules/code.md`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

## The platform's packages

**R15. `app` takes `@altnovatrail/canvas` 0.3.4, `@altnovatrail/contracts` 0.13.0 with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.16.0, connect-es 2.3.0 with the Connect transport for the browser, and Firebase Auth by the packages `@firebase/app` 0.16.5 and `@firebase/auth` 1.13.9 — those that `firebase` 13.2.0 pins. `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.13.0 is the own copy of `app`, and the canvas has its own, 0.12.0. The versions that another package decides are held by a test, and a working copy of `canvas` comes to the development server by a recipe, without touching `package.json` and the lock file.** The author's answers of 2026-11-10 in the planning of T16 and in its execution; the rest are the plan's proposals, which the author approved (RUN.md, T16, «Решено автором»). The versions — `npm view` of 2026-11-10, [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4.
- **Installation** — as R14 tells: first the third-party ones with `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, then `@altnovatrail/canvas`, `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` without a date. The lock file has 195 packages, and only four packages of `@altnovatrail` in it have a later date: the dates of all are checked against the registry. The canvas's third-party transitive packages — `rbush` 4.0.1, `quickselect` 3.0.0 and `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3 — are older than the pause. `release-verify-npm` in `ci-licenses` checked all four: `@altnovatrail/canvas` 0.3.4, signed by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.21, `@altnovatrail/contracts` 0.13.0 and `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.13.0 and 0.12.0. It runs in the job "TypeScript / licenses" on each pull request, wider than «на тот, что меняет lock-файл» (Д92 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`): no job of its own is needed.
- **The canvas is 0.3.4**, the latest as of 2026-11-10: since 0.3.0 it reads format 0.4 of the submodule's tag (T85 `canvas`). It itself stands on `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.12.0 in `dependencies`, and `app` on its own copy 0.13.0 in `devDependencies`, as R73 spec tells: «две версии одной формы — один тип, а разошедшаяся форма — ошибка компилятора на стыке `app` и `canvas`». `types/canvas` and `types/formatv0` did not change between v0.12.0 and v0.13.0 of `spec` (`git diff --stat`). The README of `canvas`, section Install, says to take `spec` "at the version the package has in its `dependencies`" — this differs from R73 spec, and the row «в реестр spec» В44 is about it (table 3 of RUN.md). The element in JSX is the augmentation `src/canvas/jsx.ts` by the types of `@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`, and the type `ElementHoldsContract` in it checks the class of the canvas element against the contract of `app`'s copy, member by member and both ways: a type that diverged is an error of `ci-lint`. In a `.d.ts` file this check would stay silent — `skipLibCheck` skips errors in it; a trial test in Chromium holds the element's registration, the properties, the attribute `lang`, the event in lower case and a type error on a tool that the contract does not know.
- **The contracts** — `@altnovatrail/contracts` 0.13.0, imported by the path of the code file, and `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.16.0 — the version that the contracts package names in `dependencies`, not 2.17.0 of 2026-11-05, which the three-day rule would give: the contracts' code and the client have one protobuf runtime. The client is `@connectrpc/connect` and `@connectrpc/connect-web` 2.3.0 (2026-10-27), `createConnectTransport`: `api` speaks Connect (R11 spec, R83 spec), and gRPC-Web is not needed. A trial test in Node writes and reads a message and calls `AttemptService` through `createRouterTransport`; the transport and its settings are T20.
- **Firebase Auth — `@firebase/app` 0.16.5 and `@firebase/auth` 1.13.9**, exactly those that `firebase` 13.2.0 of 2026-11-02 pins, in one Dependabot group. The package `firebase` 13.2.0 brought about 110 packages without `dev` into the lock file, and its `@firebase/firestore` 4.19.1 — the latest as of 2026-11-10 too — holds `@grpc/grpc-js` ~1.9.0. 1.9.16 has a high finding GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j of 2026-09-30, fixed in 1.13.6 and 1.14.5, and `ci-vuln` failed on it, though `app` does not import `firestore`, and `grpc-js` works only in Node. With two packages the lock file has 7 of them, `npm audit --omit=dev` gives no findings, and `NOTICE` does not name what is not in the build. The modular API is the same: `firebase/auth` 13.2.0 is `export * from '@firebase/auth'`, and `firebase/app` is `export * from '@firebase/app'` plus `registerVersion("firebase", "13.2.0")` — the wrapper's name and version among the versions that the SDK reports about itself. Section 3.7 is derived from the code of 13.1.0 (Д8); between `@firebase/auth` 1.13.8 and 1.13.9 the addresses of `identitytoolkit` and `securetoken`, the pages `/__/auth/handler` and `/__/auth/iframe`, the session key `firebase:authUser:<apiKey>:<app name>` and the package's exports are the same, and what changes is the margin of the token refresh — from 30 s to 5 minutes — and unhandled promise rejections ([CHANGELOG](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/main/packages/auth/CHANGELOG.md), read 2026-11-10).
- **The versions that another package decides** are held by `scripts/pins.ts`, which a test in `ci-test` runs: the icon set is the one in the `devDependencies` of the installed `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` (a package without the set is skipped); `@bufbuild/protobuf` is the one in the `dependencies` of `@altnovatrail/contracts`; each package of `@firebase` is in the lock file once, since `@firebase/auth` registers itself in the component container of `@firebase/app`; the lock file has no links to folders. Dependabot `npm` keeps `@bufbuild/protobuf` and the set in `ignore`, and `@connectrpc/*` and `@firebase/*` in groups of their own.
- **A working copy of `canvas`** (Д3) is the recipe `just dev-canvas <folder>`: it sets `NOVATRAIL_CANVAS`, and only on the development server does `vite.config.ts` point each entry point that the copy's `package.json` exports to its built files in `lib/` and let the server into the folder. The copy is built by itself, `just build` in it. `package.json` and the lock file do not change, and the build and the tests do not read the variable: only the package that `release-verify-npm` checked goes into what ships and into CI.
- **`NOTICE`** is by R14, and also: a line's license is the SPDX expression as it is, `(Apache-2.0 AND BSD-3-Clause)` for `@bufbuild/protobuf`, and each of its identifiers needs a line `Text of`; for a package that has a `NOTICE` of its own, its text goes as it is as a section `Notice of <name> <version>:` — so Apache License 2.0, clause 4(d), tells whoever distributes the work, — and a package without such a file has no section. Only `@altnovatrail/canvas` has a `NOTICE` of its own now: Material Symbols. The text of Apache-2.0 is the file `LICENSE` beside it, the same as that of `app`. The copyright of packages without a license file — `@bufbuild/protobuf`, connect-es and `@firebase/*` — is by the headers of their code. `ci-licenses` installs the packages itself: the packages' `NOTICE` is read from `node_modules`.

— *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-11-10 — the icons by a script and Firebase by the packages `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth` — by the recommendations; the second answer came during the execution, when `ci-vuln` failed on `firebase` 13.2.0, and at first the author chose the package `firebase`. The canvas, the contracts, the transport, the order of installation, the test of versions, the working copy and `NOTICE` are the plan's proposals, which the author approved.

— *Rejected:*
- `firebase` 13.2.0 — for the reason above; it with `overrides` of `@grpc/grpc-js` 1.14.5 — a version outside the range that `firestore` names, and about 110 packages whose next finding in Node code would fail `ci-vuln` again; `firebase` in T22 — the same finding, later;
- `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.12.0 for `app`, as the README of `canvas` tells: R73 spec keeps its version at the submodule's tag, and `app` needs `legalv1` and `deploymentv1` in 0.13.0; R73 spec rejected `peerDependencies`;
- `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0 — a second protobuf runtime beside the one the contracts' code is built for;
- `npm link` with the folder of `canvas` — it edits `package.json` and the lock file, and `release-verify-npm` lets a link through without a check (R77 spec); `resolve.alias` in a configuration file of its own outside `main` — a second file that is easy to forget to remove.

— *Consequence:* the table of section 3.1 — the versions of the lock file and `@iconify/utils` instead of `unplugin-icons` and `@svgr/*`; section 3.7 — Firebase 13.2.0; Д3 closed; the rows «в реестр spec» В44 and В45 go to `spec`. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.7 and 4; RUN.md, T16.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11:* `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.15.0, the same version as the submodule's tag (R26), and the canvas still has its own 0.12.0; `release-verify-npm` checked both. In 0.15.0 the part `legal/` is version 2, and the types package has `legalv2` instead of `legalv1`; `app` does not import them — it gets the texts from `api`, — and the list of countries is held by the type `Country` from `consentagev1` (R134 spec). By the same move `marked` 18.1.3 came into the build (R27), with a line in `NOTICE`. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4.

— *Second amendment 2026-11-11:* `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.15.1, the same version as the submodule's tag (R28); `@bufbuild/protobuf` is still 2.16.0, which 0.15.1 names too; `release-verify-npm` accepted both. In 0.15.1 `AnchorSkillState` has the field `teaching_modules`, and the types of `@altnovatrail/spec` are the same. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4.

— *Third amendment 2026-11-12:* the canvas is `@altnovatrail/canvas` 0.3.15, the latest on npm as of 2026-11-12, and the icon set is 1.2.98, which it names in `devDependencies`. The executor decided it in the planning of T36: the author was unavailable by a one-time instruction of 2026-11-12, and looks at the decision in the morning (RUN.md, T36, «Решено при планировании»). `release-verify-npm` accepted it: "canvas v0.3.15, signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.22".
- **Why 0.3.15 and not 0.3.13**, which Д218 names on an unmerged branch of `spec`: since 0.3.14 the canvas stands on `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.1 — the same version as the submodule's tag and `app`'s copy, — and the lock file has one copy of it, not two; 0.3.14 also brings the fixes of 3.1.2 — the name of the sheet's region and the value of a demo on the workbook in their own language (T106, T107 `canvas`). 0.3.15 only raises the development dependencies, and among them the icon set 1.2.98: the same as `@altnovatrail/skill-map` 0.1.4 and 0.1.5 have, so T43 will take the skill map without a conflict of sets (R78 spec). The set 1.2.98 came out on 2026-11-07 — older than three days before the installation (R14), — and the paths of `app`'s icons in it are the same: `scripts/icons.ts` changed only the version line in `src/app/icons.ts`.
- **One copy of `@altnovatrail/spec`.** The package is in the canvas's `dependencies`, so in the lock file it is no longer `dev`: its line in `NOTICE` is 0.15.1 instead of the canvas's former 0.12.0. `ElementHoldsContract` stays: a canvas that names another version will again install its own copy, and a type that diverged will again be an error of `ci-lint` (R73 spec).
- **Installation** — `npm install --save-exact` of the two packages; the rest of the lock file did not move, and the nested copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.12.0 left it.

— *Rejected:* 0.3.13 — a second copy of `@altnovatrail/spec`, 0.15.0, without the fixes of 3.1.2 of 0.3.14 and with the set 1.2.97, which in T43 would diverge from the skill map; 0.3.14 — the same set 1.2.97. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4; RUN.md, T36.

— *Fourth amendment 2026-11-12:* `app` takes `@altnovatrail/skill-map` 0.1.5 — the latest on npm as of 2026-11-12, of 2026-11-11, — by `npm install --save-exact`, without a date, as all packages of `@altnovatrail` (R14). Its icon set is 1.2.98, the same as the canvas's, and `scripts/pins.ts` holds it for both packages. In `dependencies` it names `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.0, and in the lock file it has a nested copy 0.15.0 beside `app`'s copy 0.15.1: only types, it puts nothing into the build, and the type `ElementTakesViews` keeps it from diverging from `app`'s copy (R73 spec). `release-verify-npm` accepted both: "skill-map v0.1.5, signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15" and "spec v0.15.0, signed by spec's release.yml". `NOTICE` has two new lines, `@altnovatrail/skill-map` 0.1.5 and `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.0, and the notice of `skill-map` itself. Why so and what was rejected — R34. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4; RUN.md, T43.

— *Fifth amendment 2026-11-12:* `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.16.3, the same version as the submodule's tag, and `@bufbuild/protobuf` is 2.17.0: the contracts package 0.16.3 names it in `dependencies` (R38). The canvas 0.3.15 now has its own nested copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.1 in the lock file, as `skill-map` 0.1.5 has 0.15.0: only types, and `ElementHoldsContract` and `ElementTakesViews` keep them from diverging from `app`'s copy (R73 spec). `release-verify-npm` accepted all six packages of `@altnovatrail` of the lock file; `NOTICE` has the lines of the contracts 0.16.3 and `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1; RUN.md, T53.

## Storage on the device: the schema and the code

**R16. `app` keeps the data on the device in four kinds of IndexedDB databases of version 1 — the device's settings, the registry, the shared cache and the database of each profile — behind a narrow interface of IndexedDB operations, for which an in-memory store stands in Node. The registry names a database before it is opened, and remembers an erasure until the browser has confirmed it; the storage's start repeats what was left unfinished, checks the sign-in project and keeps the 30-day limit.** The plan's proposals of T18, which the author approved on 2026-11-10 together with the plan (RUN.md, T18, «Решено автором»). It decides what section 3.2 and R04 left to the code epic — the names of the databases and stores and the schema version. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2, "Code", and the SDK's code in section 4.
- **Databases and stores.** All stores have keys outside the records, and an index reads one field of a record. The names have the prefix `novatrail-`, so that among the origin's databases they are seen beside the database of Firebase Auth.

  | Database | Store | Key | Record | Indexes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `novatrail-settings` | `settings` | the setting's name | `language` — the tag of the device's language | — |
  | `novatrail-registry` | `registry` | `"account"`, `"signIn"`, `"ending"` | `{ id }`; `{ projectId, apiKey }`; `true` while the sign-out waits for a database that could not be erased | — |
  | | `databases` | the database's name | `{ kind, profileId?, erase }`, where `kind` is `profile` or `cache` | — |
  | `novatrail-cache` | `views` | `[digest, view]`, the view being `manifest`, `skill-graph`, `techniques` or `solving-steps` | `{ bytes, usedAt }` — the bytes as `api` gave them | `usedAt` |
  | `novatrail-profile-<profile ID>` | `attempts` | attempt ID | `{ attemptId, digest, task?, steps?, changedAt }` | `digest`, `changedAt` |
  | | `history` | `[attempt ID, n]` | an event of the writing history, as the canvas gave it | — |
  | | `submissions` | `[attempt ID, n]` | `{ context, historyLength, work }` | — |
  | | `correctWork` | attempt ID | the work of the correct submission, until the correct answer words | — |
  | | `lessons` | lesson ID | `{ lessonId, digest, passageId, view, place, demo, changedAt }` | `digest`, `changedAt` |
- **The schema version** is 1 for all databases. Opening at a higher version brings the stores and indexes to the schema and removes the extra ones, and a migration of data, if one is needed, will come with that version. A build that meets a database newer than its own offers to reload, as after a rollout (section 3.7): a newer build opened this database. Essays, voices, the own key and the time after which the owner profile closes will come with E3 and E4 (section 3.10) — as records of the registry or a new version of the profile database.
- **The registry.** The record in `databases` goes before the database's first opening, and a profile database is opened only if the registry names it and it is not marked for erasure. An erasure sets `erase: true`, then calls `deleteDatabase`, and removes the row only on success. A sign-out erases the registry last, when everything else is erased; otherwise the registry stays with `"ending"`, and the next start finishes the job.
- **A lesson** lies under one lesson ID, and the digest and the run ID are in the record. A read with another digest or another run erases it: that is the same lesson on another package or another run under the same key (section 3.4).
