The section for adults is the screens `#/adults` in the address's hash, in the device's language: on the first page, the account's profiles and the buttons of the device's language, and each profile has a page of its own, `#/adults/profile`, whose profile is chosen in the section and lives in memory alone. The entry is the button "For grown-ups" on the choice of the learner and in the profile's header, in E3 without a PIN. The profile page is one form: the pseudonym, the default mode, the language of the learner's screens and voicing; "Save" sends `UpdateProfile` with only the fields that changed, and the other tabs learn of the profile through `BroadcastChannel`. The learner's screens are in the language of the learner's profile.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T37: the author was not available, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 the tasks T36–T48 ran one after another without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T37, «Решено при планировании»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3, 3.5, 3.8 and 3.10.
- **Routes.** `#/adults` is the section's first page, `#/adults/<page>` the others. The hash does not name the profile (R14): the page's profile is the one chosen by the button "Profile: …" on the first page, in the section's memory. The profile page when no profile is chosen — after a reload —, and a page the build does not know, lead to the first page at their own place in the history, as `#/task` without an attempt leads home.
- **Where it is.** `AccountGate` shows the section while the account is open, in the place of the choice of the learner or of the learner's screens. On the choice of the learner no learner is chosen yet, and there are no learner's screens under it. The learner's screens lose nothing on leaving: what the tab learned about the attempt and the lessons stays with the profile, as after "Switch learner" (R20, R23).
- **The section's header** is above each of its pages: "For grown-ups", "Back to the section" on any page but the first, and "Close the section". The closed section leads home: to the choice of the learner if no learner is chosen, otherwise to "My subjects".
- **The first page** is "Profiles", a button "Profile: {pseudonym}" for each profile of the account: the owner first, the others by the `Intl.Collator` of the screen's language (section 3.8); and "Language on this device", the buttons of the device's language with a line that this section and the screens before a learner is chosen are in it (R10).
- **How the section grows.** A page of the whole account or of the device is a part of the hash and a button on the first page, and a page of one profile is a part of the hash and a button on the profile page, and its profile is the one chosen in the section. So the tasks of E3 fit in; the parts of the hash are proposed here, and the tasks themselves decide them:
  - "This device", T38, — `#/adults/device`, from the first page: it is about the device, and the profiles are inside it;
  - keeping works on the server, T39, — a switch on the profile page, as T39 places it;
  - the legal pages, "Accessibility" and the notices of third-party code, T40, — T40 decides their routes: they are visible before the sign-in too, and the section's first page leads to them;
  - the licenses of modules, T41, — where they are seen in the section, T41 decides;
  - progress, T44, — `#/adults/progress`, from the profile page: progress is the profile's;
  - own key, T46, — `#/adults/own-key`, from the first page: the key is the owner profile's (R08);
  - the platform AI quota, T49, — the account's share, on the first page or as a page of its own.
- **The entry.** The button "For grown-ups" is on the choice of the learner, under the list of profiles beside "Sign out", and in the profile's header, between "Switch learner" and "Sign out": so the glossary of section 3.8 names its place. In E3 the section opens without a PIN: the account has only the owner profile, of the category `adult`, and the PIN is E4 (section 3.10). It will stand before the section in the same place where `AccountGate` opens the section.
- **The profile page** is "Profile: {pseudonym}", one form and the button "Save":
  - the pseudonym is a field with the same check as at the profile's creation: from 1 to 30 characters without spaces at the edges (R98 spec);
  - the default mode is "Step by step" or "On my own" (R05), with a line that each task begins in it, and the learner switches it on the task screen;
  - the language of the learner's screens is "English" or «Русский», each by its own name and with its own `lang` (R10). With no field, a line that the learner's screens are in the language of the device; with a language not in the build, a line with its name from `Intl.DisplayNames`. The page does not let the field be removed;
  - voicing is "Read the tutor's words aloud" and the speed "Normal" or "Slow" (R07). While there is no synthesis — until T45 —, the page says that this version does not voice yet, and the setting will be saved. And it advises whoever listens to a screen reader to turn voicing off: the two would speak at once (section 3.9).

  The choices are radio buttons, which change nothing until "Save" (WCAG 3.2.2; section 3.9). `UpdateProfile` gets only the fields the adult changed against how the form showed the profile. A value that the build reads in its own way — an unknown mode as "by steps", the tag `ru-RU` as `ru` — goes only if the adult changed it: `app` does not write back what it does not know ([the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). Whether voicing is on and its speed go apart, as `Voicing` carries them apart. When nothing changed there is no request, and the status line says "Nothing has changed"; when saved, "Saved"; a refusal or a failure is its words under the button, and the choice stays. An edit the adult made while the request was going stays an edit after the answer too: the answer removes only those that went. A failure that belongs to the session moves the session too: a sign-in that cannot be extended, and "account gone", as in the other forms (R19), and `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE` — `api` no longer has the profile, it was deleted on another device —, and `app` asks `GetAccount` anew: that erases the profile on the device (R04), and the section returns to the first page, now without it.
- **The answer and the other tabs.** The answer of `UpdateProfile` is the profile. `app` takes it whole, but for the open attempt and the lesson runs: it leaves those as the tab knows them; the profile that `GetAccount` gave stays the same for what the tab learned about it. The same profile goes to the other tabs of `app` by the `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-profiles` as a binary message `Profile`, and they do the same. A profile their account does not have, and a message that is not a profile, change nothing. The fields of the form the adult did not touch show what the other tab saved, and the touched ones stay until the adult saves their own. The answers and the messages have no order: the contract gives the profile no version, and the tab takes the profile that came last. When two tabs save one profile at once, a tab may show not what `api` saved last, until the next `GetAccount` — at the start or after a sign-in — gives it anew.
- **The language.** The section is in the device's language (R10). The learner's screens — the profile's header and the screens under it — are since T37 in the language of the learner's profile, and with no field in the language of the device, and `<html lang>` is their language while they are on the screen (section 3.8). `app` creates a new owner profile with the screen's language: "On a new profile `app` sends the language of the section for adults" ([the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; `ProfileSettings` in `account.proto`).

— *Why:*
- the address does not name the profile (R14), and without the address the profile is chosen on the first page again — just as the attempt and the lesson are chosen in "My subjects";
- the entrance is from the choice of a learner, because the glossary puts it there and in the header, and under the choice of a learner there are no learner's screens yet: a section living inside them would not open from there;
- one form with "Save", because the task orders sending only the fields that changed, and the adult changes them together. A write on each tap would give a request and a status line for each, and a failure of one field in the middle of editing others;
- from the response, everything but the attempt and the runs: the tab knows them in its own way, since `GetAccount` gave them (R20, R23), and the response must neither bring back an attempt that ended nor lose one that began. The rest is `api`'s, and a field that the contract adds to the settings comes without an edit of the list of fields;
- `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE` means a new `GetAccount`: a profile that `api` does not have is not needed on the device, and a form without a profile saves nothing;
- order without a version, since `api` does not give one, and two adults of one account saving one profile in the same second are rare in E3: the account holds one owner profile. How `api` orders simultaneous `UpdateProfile` calls is said neither by the [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, nor by `account.proto` v0.15.1 — `Profile` has neither a version nor a time of change;
- `BroadcastChannel` is what section 3.8 orders — "as of the own key". The message is a binary `Profile`: a tab of another build reads it by its own contract, and a field it does not know protobuf skips;
- the learner's screens in the profile's language, by R10 and the table of section 3.10 "What each epic takes from the decisions": E3 is "the profile's language — in the section for adults". A field that changes nothing on the screens the adult would set in vain.

— *Rejected:*
- the profile in the hash, `#/adults/profile/<ID>` — R14 forbids it;
- the section inside the learner's screens, under `App` — it would not open from the choice of a learner;
- a write on each tap, as the buttons of the device's language do — the reason above; the buttons of the device's language write only to the device;
- a `<select>` for the language or the mode — section 3.9 holds 3.2.2 with buttons and radio buttons, not with a list;
- clearing the profile's language, "as on the device" — the contract allows it, but R10 does not ask for it: the adult chooses the language, and for a profile without the field the learner's screens are in the device's language anyway;
- the whole profile from the response in place of the previous one, with the attempt and the runs — the reason about them above;
- a list of the settings fields that `app` takes from the response — a field that the contract adds would be lost without an edit of the list;
- forbidding edits while the request goes — the adult would see the form freeze, and an edit during the request stays anyway;
- in another tab, rereading `GetAccount` on the signal — its loading takes down the learner's screens, in the middle of a task too;
- "Close the section" as the browser's "Back" — a section opened by the address or after a reload would leave the pages of `app`.

— *Does not decide:* the section's PIN — E4; the pages of T38–T49 — the tasks themselves, by the layout above; the avatar — while `app` has no set of pictures; the learner's grade — with the first module whose manifest names grades (section 3.10); the children's profiles and "ask before showing" — E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/adults/` — `AdultSection.tsx` and `ProfilePage.tsx`; `src/account/settings.ts`; `Session.profileChanged` and `Session.profileFailed`, and `Session.createOwnerProfile` with the screen's language; `AccountGate` — the section, the channel and the button on the choice of a learner; `App` — "For grown-ups" in the header and `LearnerLanguage`; `src/i18n/page.ts` and `src/i18n/LearnerLanguage.tsx`; `AttemptContext` and `LessonContext` — by the profile that `GetAccount` gave; the route `ADULTS` in `src/app/hash.ts`; the keys `adults.*` in the dictionaries. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3, 3.5, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T37.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* the platform AI quota share stood not on the first page of the section and not on a page of its own, but as the part "Tutor time" at the bottom of the profile page: `GetQuota` asks for a profile and gives the share of the profile and of the account together (R40).

**R30. The adult turns the storage of works on the server on and off on the profile page in the section for adults. Turning it on is a consent in place of the button: the text `storage-consent` that `GetLegalTexts` gives in force, in the section's language, the words of R127 spec as a string of `app`, and the tick "I give my explicit consent", which holds to the version shown; `SetWorkStorage` is `ACTION_ADULT` with this version, and on `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` the text is read anew and the consent is asked again, as in R27. Turning it off is at once, without a question, with a line that the copies on the server are erased. While `api` keeps no copies of works, and the contract gives no request that would send them, the section says that storage turned on records the consent, while the works stay only on this device, and the warning of "This device" about the loss of works stands at every profile, with storage turned on too.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T39: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 tasks T36–T48 went in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T39, «Решено при планировании»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.8 and 3.10.
- **Where.** Under the settings form on the profile page `#/adults/profile`, a part of its own, "Keeping works on the server", as R29 proposed. Not a field of the form: storage has its own consent, its own request and its own response, and the settings' "Save" does not send it.
- **What is visible.** Whether storage is on — by `Profile.work_storage`, as a line with the role `status`, so that a change from this tab or from another is heard by a screen reader. Under it, a line that the server does not store works yet: storage turned on records the consent, while the works stay only on this device. It stands both with storage off — the adult learns this before the consent — and with it on.
- **Turning on.** "Keep works on the server" puts the consent in its place and gives focus to its heading:
  - the text `storage-consent` through `LegalText` from `src/legal/` — the gist in the language that `api` found, a line that the English text is in force, a translation, if the version carries one, and the body. The texts are read when the consent is opened, in the section's language — the device's language (R10) — past the HTTP cache, as in R27;
  - the words of R127 spec: "A work may tell something personal, about health and family too. Nobody reads it" — the description of the tick, `aria-describedby`;
  - the tick "I give my explicit consent to keeping this profile's works on the server, with what they tell about health" — the explicit consent to what the work reveals about health (Art. 9(2)(a) GDPR; R127 spec). It holds to the version at which it was set: if another is shown, it is cleared;
  - "Switch it on" and "Cancel". Without the tick — the field's error and focus on it, no request, and a tick set clears the error; while the text is not read, the button is disabled, and a failure to read gives its words and "Try again". While the request goes, the button is `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`: focus does not fall off it, and a second press sends nothing.

  `SetWorkStorage` is `profile_id`, `ACTION_ADULT`, `enabled` and the `LegalTextRef` of the version shown; with no text in force, without `text`, as the contract orders, and the tick with the words of R127 spec is needed all the same. `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` clears the tick, says that the text has changed, reads it anew and puts focus on the tick; while the text is being read, the previous one stays on the screen. Another refusal or failure — its words under the buttons, and the consent stays open and ticked. After the response the consent goes, and focus goes to the button that took its place, "Stop keeping works on the server"; after "Cancel", to "Keep works on the server". Storage that another tab turned on while the consent is open here closes the consent — with focus on "Stop keeping…" — and the consent does not come back by itself afterwards.
- **Turning off** — "Stop keeping works on the server", at once, without a question and without a text: withdrawal is as easy as giving (Art. 7(3) GDPR; R113 spec). The button's description says that storage is turned off at once, the copies of this profile's works on the server, if there are any, are erased, and the works on this device stay: "if there are any" so that the description does not argue with the line that the server does not store works yet ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "The storage of works"). While the request goes, the button is `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`: focus does not fall off it.
- **The response** is the profile, and its path is the same as that of `UpdateProfile` (R29): `Session.profileChanged` and the `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-profiles` to the other tabs. A failure that belongs to the session moves it too — `Session.profileFailed`.
- **While there are no copies.** `api` writes the consent but keeps no copies of works (Д12 `api`, read on 2026-11-12 on `main` of `api`, 77fbf21: «С T43 включение и выключение хранения у профиля и согласие на него — в коде, а копий работ сервер ещё не держит» (R52 `api`)). Nor is there anything to send them with: in contract v0.15.1 there is no request that gives a work for storage, reads it back or erases one — `SubmitWork` sends a crop to be checked, and the server does not store it ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; `account.proto`, `attempt.proto`). So `copiedOnServer` in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx` is still "no" at every profile, and the line "the server does not store works yet" stands in the storage part. They change together, when the contract gives a request and `api` starts keeping copies: the question is В53, the debt is Д16 in RUN.md.
- **A learner with their own consent** — E4. Their storage they decide themselves, `ACTION_LEARNING` (R136 spec), and to an adult action `api` refuses with `CAUSE_LEARNER_DECIDES`: the section says this in the words of the refusal, and builds no view of its own for such a profile in E3 — the account has only the owner profile, `CONSENT_GIVER_OWNER`. The screen for a teenager whose storage an adult turned on is R141 spec, E4 too.

— *Why:*
- the text of the consent is a legal text with a version, not a string of `app` (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R127 spec; R131 spec); but the tick needs its own label, and R127 spec names what the switch says. The label and the description of the tick say it as a string of `app`, and what binds is the text whose version the consent record writes. Without a text in force — a self-hosting copy without `storage-consent` — the words of R127 spec stay on the screen;
- the consent in place of the button, not on a page of its own, like the disclosures of the texts on the invite code screen (R27): the adult does not leave the profile page, and focus and return are as with the question before erasing (R04; section 3.9);
- a tick and a button, not one button "I agree": explicit consent is a separate affirmative act, and the invite code screen is built the same way (R27);
- the line "the server does not store works yet", because the text `storage-consent` speaks of storage "so that they are not lost", and now it saves nothing. An adult who turned storage on so as not to lose works must know that they are still only here (R113 spec; RULES.md, The bar). Recording the consent now is no more data: the record is who, when and the text version (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R113 spec);
- the warning of "This device" stands at every profile: without a copy on the server the work exists only here, whatever `work_storage` says (R113 spec, "Until then, and in a profile where storage is not turned on, … warn of the loss…").

— *Rejected:*
- not letting storage be turned on while there are no copies — the consent, the text version and the refusals would go to the first essay of T47 unchecked, and the adult could not give consent in advance; recording the consent adds no data;
- only the text `storage-consent`, without a string of `app` about the copies — the text promises a safekeeping that does not exist;
- the words of R127 spec instead of the text, as a string of `app` — the consent record does not hold the version of a string of `app` (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R127 spec);
- the consent on a page of its own, `#/adults/storage` — leaving the profile page for the sake of one switch; R29 puts it on the profile page;
- a question before turning off — withdrawal must be as easy as giving, and the task plainly orders «снятие согласия не спрашивает»;
- removing the warning of "This device" with storage turned on — there is no copy on the server.

— *Does not decide:* how a work gets to the server, is read back and is erased one by one — В53 «в реестр spec»; a learner with their own consent and the screen of R141 spec — E4; "Erase" at an essay — T47.

— *Consequence:* `src/adults/WorkStorage.tsx` — the part and the consent; `ProfilePage` — the part under the form; `STORAGE_TEXT` and `storageText` in `src/legal/texts.ts`; the comment of `copiedOnServer` in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`; the keys `storage.*` in the dictionaries. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T39, В53 and Д16.

## Legal pages

**R31. The legal pages are screens of their own, `#/legal` in the address's hash, in the device's language, visible both before sign-in and after: a list and a page each for the privacy policy, the terms of use and the consent to the storage of works — the texts that `GetLegalTexts` gives in force, open — for "Accessibility" and for the notices of third-party code. They are reached by the button "Privacy and legal information" on the sign-in screen and the button "Read the privacy policy and other texts" on the first page of the section for adults. The page of notices takes `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` from the bundle, where the build puts them, not from the hosting's `/NOTICE`. About accessibility, people write to the operator of the copy: how to reach the operator the privacy policy says, and a button of the page leads to it. A new version of a text before it applies contract v0.15.1 does not give — this is the row «в реестр spec» В54 and the debt Д17 in RUN.md.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T40: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 tasks T36–T48 went in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision, and proofreads the texts of the page "Accessibility" line by line (RUN.md, T40, «Решено при планировании»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.8, 3.9 and 3.10.
- **Routes.** `#/legal` is the list, `#/legal/<page>` the pages: `privacy-policy`, `terms-of-use` and `storage-consent` by the text's ID, `accessibility` and `notices`. A page that the build does not know leads to the list in its own place in the history, like an unknown page of the section (R29). The texts for the family — `parent-consent`, `teen-consent`, `child-notice` and `teen-notice` — get no pages of their own: the child consent screens show them, E4.
- **Where.** `AccountGate` shows the pages at any step of the session — before sign-in, on the choice of a learner and in place of the learner's screens — before the section for adults. They need only the client of `api`, and it exists before sign-in: `GetLegalTexts` goes without a token (R27). The language is the device's (R10): the pages are opened by the sign-in screen and by the section, both in the device's language, and the table of section 3.8 gives "Accessibility" the language of the screen it was opened from.
- **The header** is above each page: "Privacy and legal information", "Back to the list" on any page but the list, and "Close" — to where they were opened from: from the sign-in screen, home, that is, to sign-in again, and from the section, to its first page. Where they were opened from `AccountGate` remembers only in memory, like the section's profile (R29): after a reload "Close" leads home. And it leads to the section only while its account is open: if the account signed out while the pages are open — in another tab or with a revoked token — "Close" leads home, otherwise the next sign-in would land right in the section.
- **The button with the word "privacy".** Д204 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` orders a link with the word "privacy" on the start screen and in the settings (CGS §42-520(b)(2), as read by `spec` on 2026-11-11). The start screen before sign-in is the sign-in screen: under the sign-in button, "Privacy and legal information", «Конфиденциальность и правовая информация». The settings are the first page of the section for adults: the heading "Privacy and legal information", a line saying what is there, and the button "Read the privacy policy and other texts", «Прочитать политику конфиденциальности и другие тексты». On a Russian screen the word is «конфиденциальность»: a string of `app` is in the screen's language (R10). It is a button, not an `<a>` link: so every move between screens in `app` is built — a button as an HTML element, a target of 44 × 44 and a label on it (section 3.9) — and it leads the same way.
- **A text page** is a heading in the screen's language, "Version {version}, in force since {date}" by `in_force_since`, with `Intl.DateTimeFormat` of the screen's language — and a day not in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`, or one that is not in the calendar, like 2026-02-30, the page writes as it came — and what the text carries, open: the gist with its own `lang`, a line that the English text is in force, the translation "for reading only", if the version carries one, and the English body, `lang="en"`, whose headings start at the second level. This is the same as the disclosure of the invite code screen (R27), without `<details>`: `LegalContent` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx`. The text is read at each opening of the page, in the screen's language and past the HTTP cache (R27). No version in force gives "This copy of the app has no version of this text in force", as with an `api` without `LegalService`; a failure gives its words and "Try again".
- **"Accessibility"** is strings of the dictionaries of `app`, properties of the build, the same in all copies (section 3.9): the standard is WCAG 2.2 AA, and beyond it 2.5.5 and 2.3.3; the places where `app` does not meet it yet are the set of the 2026-11-12 amendment to R11: the answer in handwriting — 2.1.1, 2.5.1 and 2.5.7, the print of the workbook's lines — 1.4.12, sending by "Check" — 3.3.4 — each with its criteria, why, what there is instead and when this changes; how it is checked — by the automated checks of CI, while checks by people with screen readers have not been done yet (Д10); and whom to turn to — the operator of this copy, whose contacts are in the privacy policy, with the button "Open the privacy policy" and a request not to write a child's personal data, as the policy itself asks.
- **The notices of third-party code** are the `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` of this build as they are, each in a `<pre lang="en">` with long lines wrapped, so that at a width of 320 px the page does not slide sideways. Their copy `src/legal/notices.ts` takes from the repository's files as text at build time — Vite's `?raw` import — a test in Node compares it with the files byte for byte, and with the same files `scripts/release.ts` compares the archive's `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` (R25). The page sends no request of its own.
- **Consent withdrawal for an owner without children.** The owner's own learning rests on the contract, Art. 6(1)(b) GDPR, not on consent (R116 spec), and accepting the policy and the terms at `CreateAccount` is not an Art. 6(1)(a) consent that is withdrawn: leaving the contract is deleting the account, and deletion is E4. The consent that such an owner gives and can withdraw is, in E3, one — the storage of works on the server (R113 spec), and the profile page removes it at once, without a question (R30). So the screen of a new version of the policy, when the contract gives it (В54), will offer an owner without children to turn storage off at the profiles where it is on, and from E4 to delete the account too.

— *Why:*
- `#/legal` is outside the section for adults: the pages are needed before sign-in (Д204 spec; architecture 3.13 spec), and the section lives only while the account is open (R29);
- a copy of `NOTICE` in the bundle, not the hosting's `/NOTICE`: `app` loads no parts of its own at run time — it gathers everything of its own into the build (R16 spec; architecture 3.1 spec), and from the hosting it reads only `/deployment.json`, which is not part of the build (R09). `/NOTICE` is part of the build, and a page that read it would depend on the hosting: `vite preview` does not serve it — it is not in `build/`, only in the archive — a self-hosting copy might not put it out, and the browser checks no bytes — the rollout of `infra` compares `/NOTICE` with the build, and self-hosting is not bound to. The CSP would allow both paths — `connect-src 'self'` (section 3.7) — but a copy in the bundle needs no request at all. The price is about 20 KB of text in the bundle. Apache-2.0 names such a place itself, in clause 4(d): "within a display generated by the Derivative Works, if and wherever such third-party notices normally appear" (the repository's `LICENSE`);
- the operator's contact is through the policy: contract v0.15.1 has no method that would give a contact (`legal.proto`; [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15), and the operator's name and contacts are in the texts that the `api` of a copy gives from its operator (R115, R131 spec): the terms refer to them so too — "its contacts are in "Who we are" of our privacy policy" (`terms-of-use` of version 1). A field of its own in the build or in `deployment.json` for this R115 spec rejected. Whether this holds at every self-hosting copy the row «в реестр spec» В55 asks;
- the list is all five buttons without reading the texts: it does not wait for the network, and a text page itself says that there is no text in force;
- the texts for the family without pages of their own — their place is at the consent screens of E4, and a page of a text that there is nothing to give yet would only confuse.

— *Rejected:*
- `/NOTICE` and `/LICENSE` from the hosting's origin — the reasons above;
- the pages inside the section for adults, `#/adults/legal` — they could not be opened before sign-in;
- a page for each text that `GetLegalTexts` gives — the family's texts without the screens of E4;
- the text as a closed disclosure `<details>`, as on the invite code screen — the page has no other business, and a closed text would only hide it;
- a link `<a href="#/legal">` instead of a button — the reason above;
- "Close" as the browser's "Back" — R29 rejected this for the section too: pages opened by the address would leave the pages of `app`;
- the English "Privacy" on a Russian screen — a string of `app` is in the screen's language (R10); if the laws of the states require the English word in other languages too, the author will say so when proofreading the strings;
- showing a new version of the policy at the first start after it came into force — this is not "before it applies", as the part "Changes to this policy" of version 2 promises and §42-520(b)(3) orders, as Д204 spec reads it.

— *Does not decide:* a new version of a text before it applies — В54 «в реестр spec», and while there is none, Д17; the operator's contact as a field of the contract — В55 «в реестр spec»; the words of the page "Accessibility" — the author proofreads them; account deletion and the family's consents — E4; the licenses of modules — T41.

— *Consequence:* `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx` — the list, the pages and the header; `src/legal/notices.ts` — the build's `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`; `LegalContent` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx`; `PAGE_TEXTS` in `src/legal/texts.ts`; the route `LEGAL` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the pages at any step and where they were opened from; the button on the sign-in screen and on the first page of the section; `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` in `server.fs.allow` of `vite.config.ts`; the keys `legal.*` and `accessibility.*` in the dictionaries. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.8, 3.9 and 3.10; RUN.md, T40, В54, В55 and Д17.

**R32. A module's license is a page of `app` of its own: the `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of the device part of one version as they are, in `<pre lang="en">`, with the module by its manifest above them — the name, the version and the license as an SPDX identifier. `app` takes the files from `api` by a request without a token by the package digest, and does not open them by a link. The page is reached by the button "License" at each card of "My subjects" with a current version — the route `#/license/<digest>`, visible at any step — and by the profile's modules page in the section for adults, `#/adults/modules`, from the profile page — the route `#/adults/license/<digest>`, a page of the section.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the execution of T41: the task has no planning, and where the page is in the section for adults R29 left to the task itself; the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 tasks T36–T48 went in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T41, «Сделано»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.8 and 3.10.
- **What is on the page.** The header "Licenses of modules" with "Close" outside the section, or the section's header in it; the heading "The module's license and notices", a line that the module's author gives it under this license; the module by its manifest — the name with its own `lang`, the version and the license; "License (LICENSE)" and "Notices (NOTICE)" — by the headings of the page of notices of third-party code (R31), but the license first: the page is about it — each as text as it is. The page reads the manifest apart from the files: a manifest that takes long or does not come does not hold up the texts, and the module is then not named.
- **Responses** ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "Files of a device part"). `200` is UTF-8 text, as the part carries it (R62 spec). `404` for `NOTICE` is "The module has no file NOTICE": `NOTICE` is not required in a package. `404` for `LICENSE` is "No file LICENSE came with the module": the format builds no package without it, and the page does not pretend it is there. `410` is "This version of the module is no longer given", and `CheckPackages` is asked, as at any `410` (R63 spec); the files are read one after another, `LICENSE` first, so that for a withdrawn version `410` comes once and `CheckPackages` is asked once. `503` with `Retry-After` — a retry after the named time, no longer than 30 s, and a network failure — and a response body broken off on the way — a retry after the client's growing pauses, and then its words and "Try again". This is the existing `src/packages/files.ts` since T21 and its text call.
- **Without a link.** The file's address is the origin of `api`, another origin, and `app` has no external links (privacy 1 and 7 spec); an `<a>` would also lead away from the pages of `app`. The response goes through the browser's HTTP cache, like media files, and `app` does not store it (section 3.2).
- **`#/license/<digest>`** is shown by `AccountGate` at any step, like the legal pages (R31): the files and the manifest need no token (R03 api), the page survives a reload, and the test of the built page checks it under the CSP header before sign-in. "Close" leads home — to "My subjects", while a learner is chosen. The language is that of the screen the page leads back to: while a learner is chosen, their interface's (R10), otherwise the device's. The package digest in the address is an ID of content, not of a learner (R14); a route without a digest leads home in its own place in the history.
- **In the section** — the profile page, as R29 orders for the page of one profile: "Modules" under the storage of works and the button "See the modules and their licenses" to `#/adults/modules` — the profile's connected modules by `ListConnectedModules`, as cards, as "My subjects" names them (`src/subjects/CardHeading.tsx`), by name in the order of the screen's language; at a module with a current version, "License", and at a module without one, "Not available now" without a button: files cannot be asked for without a digest. The license in the section is its page `#/adults/license/<digest>`, in the device's language, under the section's header and with "Back to the modules" while a profile is chosen; after a reload, without a chosen profile, only the header. Without a profile, the modules page leads to the section's first page, like the profile page (R29).
- **`lang="en"`** on the texts, as the task orders: the official modules are Apache-2.0 in English (R20 spec). In which language the texts of a third-party module are the format does not say, and which licenses it may have is О-10; this is the row «в реестр spec» В56 in RUN.md.

— *Why:*
- Apache-2.0, clause 4(a), orders giving the recipient a copy of the license — "You must give any other recipients of the Work or Derivative Works a copy of this License" (R62 spec) — and with the deployment the package travels to others (RUN.md, T41). The page of `app` gives it where the module is visible, and without going to another origin;
- one route outside the section, not a page inside the learner's screens: "My subjects" opens it with one button, it is visible after a reload and before sign-in, and its header is the same as that of the legal pages;
- a page of its own in the section, not the same `#/license/…`: the section lives in the device's language and under its own header (R29), and "Close" would lead from the section home. The profile page's own part of the hash and a button on it are the path that R29 gave the page of one profile;
- connected modules, not the catalog: what is connected travels to the learner's device, and the modules of the catalog have their license on their card in "My subjects". `ListConnectedModules` is a learning request with the right `can_learn` ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "Modules"): in E3 the account has only the owner profile, and this is its right;
- the card is shared with "My subjects", so that a module is named the same in both places: the name from the manifest of the current version, the explanation language, "Not available now".

— *Rejected:*
- a link to `/packages/…/device/LICENSE` at `api` — the reason above;
- a copy of the modules' licenses in the bundle, like the build's `NOTICE` (R31) — the modules come from `api` after the build, and the build does not know their versions;
- a page in the learner's screens under their header — it could not be opened from the section, and after a reload it would wait for the choice of a learner;
- one route for the section too, with "Close" by memory, as on the legal pages (R31) — then the language and the header would depend on the tab's memory, not on the address;
- the licenses of the modules that the device's cache holds instead of the profile's modules — the cache holds manifests per module, without the digest of each version, and is empty before sign-in, while the adult looks for the learner's module;
- `lang` by the manifest on the texts of the license — the module's explanation language is not the language of its license.

— *Does not decide:* in which language the `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of a third-party module are — В56 «в реестр spec», together with О-10; the licenses of the catalog's modules in the section for adults — if an adult who does not learn themselves needs to see them before connecting, this is a catalog page in the section, with the children's profiles in E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/packages/LicensePage.tsx` — the page, `licenseRoute` and `LicensePages`; `src/packages/license.ts` — `readLicense`; `text` and `isDigest` in `src/packages/files.ts`; `src/adults/ModulesPage.tsx` — `MODULES_PAGE`, `sectionLicense` and the modules page; `src/subjects/CardHeading.tsx`; the button "License" in `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`; "Modules" in `src/adults/ProfilePage.tsx`; the pages `modules` and `license` in `src/adults/AdultSection.tsx`; the route `MODULE_LICENSE` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the page at any step, in the learner's language while one is chosen; the keys `license.*`, `subjects.license` and `adults.modules*` in the dictionaries. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T41 and В56.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14:* the format of `spec` v0.17.1 says in which language a module's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` are: the language of the license, not the module's explanation language (T147 `spec`; `format/README.md`, «The language of the license»), — the answer to the row В56. `lang="en"` holds for the official modules, which are Apache-2.0 in English; a third-party module under a license in another language waits for О-10, which licenses it may carry (R43).

## Tutor — AI

**R33. That the tutor is an AI `app` always says where its words stand: by the line "The tutor is an AI, not a person" under the heading of each block "Tutor", and in "My subjects" by the part "The tutor is an AI" under the lists, with a button to the learning materials. The learning materials on safe use are a page of `app` of its own, `#/tutor`, "How to use the AI tutor safely", in `en` and `ru`, visible at any step, in the language of the learner's screens while a learner is chosen, and without external links. "Report a problem" is a button under the tutor's words at each of its replies — a rung, "Explain differently", the correct answer words, and from T47 the feedback by a rubric too — and a form in its place: one of six kinds of problem, a text of up to 1000 characters with a request not to write names, the words as the block showed them, `ReportProblem` with one idempotency key per report, and the answer "It will be reviewed by …" by `review_by`. The button is seen by a learner of the category `adult` — in E3 that is the adult of the account; a profile of another category does not have it until E4 decides by П-26.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T42: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 tasks T36–T48 went in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision and proofreads the texts of the disclosure and of the materials (RUN.md, T42). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.8 and 3.10.
- **The disclosure — where and when.** The line stands in the block "Tutor" whenever the block is there: at the words of a rung, at "Explain differently", at the correct answer words and while "The tutor is writing…". That is the minute when the learner reads the AI's words. "My subjects" — the home screen, through which the learner goes to a task — says the same by a part under the lists and leads to the materials. Not once "at the first meeting", but always: an "already seen" mark would lie on the device and would be lost with erasing, on another device and in a private window. The block "Hint" before a wrong answer is the module's content, without AI (R82 spec), and has no line. What the providers require, read on 2026-11-12:
  - Anthropic, [Guidelines for organizations serving minors](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/9307344-responsible-use-of-anthropic-s-models-guidelines-for-organizations-serving-minors), updated 2026-03-16: "Organizations must disclose to their users that they are interacting with an AI system rather than a human"; [Usage Policy](https://www.anthropic.com/legal/aup), "Effective Nov 12, 2026": "All consumer-facing chatbots or AI agents that communicate directly with external users must disclose to users that they are interacting with AI rather than a human";
  - OpenAI, [Under-18 API guidance](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/safety-checks/under-18-api-guidance), no date on the page: "Providing age-appropriate disclosures to minors about AI tools and how to use them responsibly".
- **The learning materials — the content.** The terms require materials but do not say what is in them: Anthropic — "Educational resources and guidance for minors on safe and responsible use of the product" (the same article), OpenAI — the line above; Anthropic's [Child Safety Guidance for Developers](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15591275-child-safety-guidance-for-developers), updated 2026-06-26, says nothing about materials for learners. OpenAI's [Usage Policies](https://openai.com/policies/usage-policies/) were not read on 2026-11-12: the site answered 403, and the Wayback Machine copy is unavailable to the reading tool — and the materials do not rest on them; their reading is R123 spec, a copy of 2026-11-06. So the content is what the platform's documents already say about the tutor, in its own words for the learner, in six parts:
  - the tutor is an AI, not a person, and whether an answer is right a program decides by the task's key (R03 spec; `privacy-policy` of version 2, "Where AI comes in");
  - it can be wrong, and work that cannot be read it asks to write again (`terms-of-use` of version 1); "Explain differently" is once per rung (R82 spec);
  - what goes to the AI and what never goes, and the picture shows everything on the sheet — so no names and nothing else personal (privacy 5.1 spec; R127 spec). Which providers the tutor calls the page does not name, but refers to the privacy policy: a self-hosting copy has its own texts (R115 spec), and with an own key there is its own provider (privacy 5.2 spec);
  - it is for learning: it gives hints and does not solve the task (R82 spec);
  - it is not someone to talk to about personal things: about health, family and feelings, talk with a person one can trust, and if the tutor asks for something personal or says something frightening, stop and tell (R123 spec, "monitoring"; R127 spec);
  - if something is wrong, "Report a problem"; the operator reviews within 3 days, the words and the text are stored as written, and in danger now, call the emergency service without waiting for the review (the 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 amendments to R123 spec).
- **One text for all learners**, while all of them are adults. In Russian it does not address the learner, like all the learner's screens (section 3.8), and the lines are short. Texts by age — OpenAI's "age-appropriate" — will come with the children's screens in E4, beside `child-notice` and `teen-notice` (R132 spec).
- **Where the page is** — a route of its own, `#/tutor`, like a module's license (R32): `AccountGate` shows it at any step, before sign-in too — there is nothing about anyone in it — in the language of the learner's screens while a learner is chosen, otherwise the device's, with the header "About the tutor" and "Close" home. A part of the hash the page does not know leads to the page itself in its own place in the history. The entrance is the button "How to use the tutor safely" in "My subjects".
- **The form** is in place of the button "Report a problem", under the words, with focus on its heading; "Cancel" brings back the button and focus to it. The kind is radio buttons in the contract's order, and without a kind the report does not go; "Something else" goes only with a text: "Something else, which the text says" (`ProblemKind` in `attempt.proto`). The text is `maxLength` 1000: the field counts UTF-16 code units, and a text has no fewer of them than the code points that the contract counts; spaces at the edges are trimmed. Under the field, a request to write no names, neither one's own nor others'; below, that the tutor's words will go with the report and that in danger now one must call the emergency service.
- **The tutor's words and reply** come from one state of the task screen: what the block shows is what goes, with the reply that brought it. The words of `GetRungWords` and the hints after a wrong answer are `TUTOR_REPLY_RUNG` with the rung's number from the response; the words with which the attempt went on after a reload are too, with the number from `RungHint`: so the contract names them, although after "Explain differently" the attempt already holds its words; "Explain differently" is `TUTOR_REPLY_EXPLAINED_DIFFERENTLY` with the number of the rung whose words it replaced; after a correct answer, `TUTOR_REPLY_CORRECT_ANSWER`, without a rung. A "Hint" that gave the same words once more leaves them their previous reply and the begun form; if "Explain differently" did not find the words of a rung on the screen, the number is by the count of wrong answers, from 1 to 3. Other words are another form: the previous one, open or sent, goes together with its words.
- **The key** is `idempotencyKey()` from `src/api/keys.ts`, one per report as the form holds it. A retry — by the client at `unavailable` or by the button after a failure — goes with the same key, and `api` answers with the same report, without creating a second ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). Another kind or another text is another report, with a new key: the same key with another request would get `CAUSE_KEY_ALREADY_USED`. A refusal ends the report, and the next "Send" is with a new key. `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` the client waits out itself, up to 30 s, and then the screen says "No connection", and the key stays.
- **The answer** is "Thank you: the report is sent. It will be reviewed by {date}": the day of `review_by` by the device's clock, in the long date of the screen's language; without `review_by`, without a day. Focus is on this line, and the button is no longer at these words; at new words it is there again.
- **Who sees the button** — a learner of the category `adult`: in E3 there are no others, and this learner is the "adult of the account" whose report R123 spec names. A profile of another category has no button, but has the disclosure and the materials. How an adult reports at a tutor's reply to a child, and whether a teenager reports themselves, E4 will decide together with П-26 (section 2): the method is a learning request (R136 spec), so the answer is to whom `app` shows the button.

— *Why:*
- the disclosure and the materials are measures that R123 spec assigns to `app` for all learners, and "report a problem" is a measure of the platform itself beyond the providers' terms; without them invite codes do not go to other adults (the author's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of E3);
- the line in the block is visible exactly when the learner reads the AI's words, and stores nothing;
- the materials say only what the platform's documents have already decided: facts about providers that cannot be checked against the primary source on that day do not appear on the page;
- the form in place, not a page of its own: leaving the task screen would lose the words after a correct answer — the server does not store them (R87 spec) — and the attempt is already closed;
- a key per report, not per press — the same technique as for the check with its work (section 3.3): a retry does not create a second report, and an edit is already another report.

— *Rejected:*
- the disclosure once, by a screen at the first meeting with the tutor — a mark on the device gets lost, while the line in the block is always visible;
- the materials among the legal pages `#/legal/…` — those are in the device's language and close to where they were opened from by the tab's memory (R31), while the materials are for the learner, in the language of the learner's screens;
- a button to the materials in the block "Tutor" — the same leaving of the task screen as for the form as a page of its own;
- the form as a page of its own or a window over the screen — the reason above, and the focus, which would leave the task screen;
- the button for all learners now — R123 spec gives it to the adult, and who has it at a child's profile П-26 decides;
- words of its own for `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` — the client waits it out itself up to 30 s, and it reaches the screen as "No connection";
- counting code points instead of `maxLength` — a field with a limit is simpler and lets nothing extra through, and it is too strict only for a text made of characters outside the BMP alone.

— *Does not decide:* the texts of the materials by age — E4, with the children's screens; the button at the profile of a child and of a teenager — E4, by П-26; "Report a problem" at the feedback by a rubric — T47: `reportRequest` already knows `TUTOR_REPLY_ESSAY_FEEDBACK`.

— *Consequence:* `src/tutor/report.ts` — `Said`, `KINDS`, `TEXT_LIMIT`, `unsendable` and `reportRequest`; `src/tutor/ReportProblem.tsx` — the button and the form; `src/tutor/TutorPage.tsx` — the page and `TutorPages`; `src/task/TutorWords.tsx` — the line of the disclosure and the place for the button; `src/task/TaskScreen.tsx` — the property `report`; `src/task/Task.tsx` — the words with their reply and rung, `Said`, and the button for a learner `adult`; `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx` — the part "The tutor is an AI"; the route `TUTOR` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the page at any step; the keys `tutor.*` and `report.*` in the dictionaries; `.nt-report` and the field `textarea` in `theme.css`. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T42.

## Skill map

**R34. The learner's "Skill map" is the screens `#/skill-map` — the list of maps of the connected modules — and `#/skill-map/<module ID>` — the module's map by the element `skill-map` 0.1.5 with `view="learner"` in the language of the learner's screens; the entrance is the button "Skill map" in "My subjects" under "What's next". "Practise" on the map sends `ChooseNext` with the skill by the same path as "What's next", through the shared hook `useEngine`, and while an attempt is open, it first asks as "Something else" does. The modules that teach an anchor "unknown" `app` shows beside the map — by the part "Modules to connect" above it, as one list without repeats, with the name from the manifest of the current version and "Connect". The mark "sequential" is the line "In order" on the module's card, by the `skill_tiers` of the current version. The map's theme is the background, the text and the primary color of the theme of `app`.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T43: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 tasks T36–T48 went in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T43). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.4, 3.8 and 3.10, and the versions are in the fourth 2026-11-12 amendment to R15.
- **The version of `skill-map`** is 0.1.5, the latest in npm as of 2026-11-12. Its icon set is 1.2.98, as for the canvas 0.3.15 (the third 2026-11-12 amendment to R15), so there is one set (R78 spec). The `@altnovatrail/spec` under it is 0.15.0: there is no release on 0.15.1, and the lock file holds a second, nested copy. It is only the types `formatv0/open` and the element's contract, and the packages 0.15.0 and 0.15.1 differ only in `package.json` (compared by `diff -r` of the unpacked packages on 2026-11-12); `ElementTakesViews` in `src/skills/jsx.ts` compares the element's properties with the types of `app`'s copy both ways, and a type that diverged is an error of `ci-lint` (R73 spec). The README of `skill-map` asks to install beside it the `@altnovatrail/spec` of the version it names, but `app`'s copy is 0.15.1, by the submodule's tag (R28): with 0.15.0 the types of `app` would diverge from the submodule and the contracts of 0.15.1, and there are no differences in the types. The copy goes when `skill-map` moves to 0.15.1 or `app` to its tag.
- **Routes** — `#/skill-map` and `#/skill-map/<module ID>`: the module ID is an ID of content, not of a learner (R14). The list is the connected modules as cards of "My subjects" with "Open the map" at a module with a current version; the map is the heading "Skill map: {module}", a hint how to choose a skill, and the element with the headings of the bands as `h2`. The buttons "All skill maps" and "Back to My subjects" lead back.
- **The map's data** is `GetSkillMap` with `ACTION_LEARNING`, and the graph with tiers and the manifest are open views from the shared cache by the digest from its response (R99 spec), so that the map and the state are on one version. A module without a graph has no map, and the screen says so.
- **Choosing a skill** is `ChooseNext` with the skill's full ID, as the event `skillchoose` sends it, and then as with "What's next": the lesson opens, the task begins (R24). The code of the choice is the hook `useEngine` in `src/next/`, shared with `NextStep`; the idempotency key is one per skill until a response or a refusal. While an attempt is open, "Practise" first asks in the same words as "Something else", and "Cancel" and "Go on" return focus to "Practise". While the choice goes, the status line says "Opening what's next…", and another press does nothing: `app` cannot disable a button inside the element. `api` holds the skill (R107 spec); "What's next" and "Something else" in "My subjects" send the choice without a skill, and it returns to the engine.
- **Modules for an anchor — beside the map** (Д211 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`): the element does not read `teaching_modules`, and `app` does. The part is above the map, right under the hint, so that it is seen before a long map. In it are the modules of all anchors whose mastery is "unknown" or a value the build does not know (so the contract orders) — each once, by name. The name is from the manifest of the current version by the digest from `ListCatalog`: the same as the catalog of "My subjects". A module that the catalog does not give the profile is "Not available now" without a button: `ConnectModule` would reject it. After "Connect", the line "Connected: it is in My subjects now" with focus, and the map's state is read anew in place.
- **The theme** — `--novatrail-skill-map-paper`, `-ink` and `-accent` — is `--nt-background`, `--nt-text` and `--nt-primary`: their pairs already hold a contrast of 4.5 : 1 in the theme's test (R11, R17), and the map looks like the screens of `app`.
- **The mark "sequential"** is by R06: `isSequential` by the `skill_tiers` of the open view of the current version's graph, from the shared cache, as the line "In order" in the header of the module's card — in "My subjects", in the list of maps and in the section for adults. The cards do not wait for the graphs: the mark appears when the graph has come, by the hook `useSequential` — and a graph that has not come gives no mark.

— *Why:*
- the button in "My subjects" — the learner's home screen and the place of the second entrance (product 4 spec), and the list of maps before the map — a learner may have several modules, and the element draws one;
- one code of the choice for both entrances: `ChooseNext` with a skill and without differs by one field, and the start of a task and of a lesson, the retry with a key and `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` are shared (R24);
- the modules beside the map do not wait for a new release of `skill-map` and a decision of `spec`, and the learner sees the anchor on the map too, in its band of anchors, with the mastery "unknown";
- the name from the manifest of the current version is what the learner will see in "My subjects" too after connecting.

— *Rejected:*
- npm `overrides` of `@altnovatrail/spec` to 0.15.1 under `skill-map`: against what the package names, the same reason that R15 gave against replacing `@grpc/grpc-js`: the element is checked by its tests on 0.15.0, not on 0.15.1;
- waiting for `skill-map` on 0.15.1: there is no such release, and the copy is only types;
- modules by anchors — the anchor's heading and its modules: `app` has no names of anchors — they are in the shared taxonomy of the format's release and built into the element, not exported — and an anchor's ID is not a word for a learner;
- a row «в реестр spec» so that the element itself shows the modules for an anchor: the element, without the network and without `ConnectModule`, would call `app` anyway, and Д211 already gives this to `app`;
- the part under the map — a long map would take it off the screen, and at 320 px off several;
- choosing a skill without a question with an open attempt — `ChooseNext` would close it silently (R24).

— *Does not decide:* the progress in the section for adults — T44: `readModuleMap` with `ACTION_ADULT` and `ModuleSkillMap` with `view="adult"` already exist; a line for a child that an adult sees their progress — E4; ranks on the map — О-13 and `skill-map`.

— *Consequence:* `src/skills/` — `jsx.ts`, `map.ts`, `SkillMap.tsx`, `SkillMapScreen.tsx`; `src/next/useEngine.ts`; `isSequential` and `sequentialOf` in `src/subjects/modules.ts` and the hook `src/subjects/useSequential.ts`; the route `SKILL_MAP` in `src/app/hash.ts`; the keys `skillmap.*` and `subjects.sequential` in the dictionaries; the theme's variables in `src/theme/theme.css`. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.4, 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T43.

## Progress in the section for adults

**R35. A profile's progress is a page of the section for adults, `#/adults/progress`, reached from the profile page: the skill maps of the connected modules as cards with "Open the map", and a module's map is a page of its own, `#/adults/progress/<module ID>`, the element `skill-map` with `view="adult"` by `GetSkillMap` with `ACTION_ADULT`; the repeating traps come from the first page of `GetProgress`; the attempts, the newest first, page by page with the button "Show more". An attempt shows its task and lesson by their local IDs, the module, the version, the outcome, the wrong answers, when it was begun and closed, and why it was given: the words of the reason code and when it was chosen, and without a choice, "began it again". The words of the nine codes are `app`'s, and a code the build does not know gets the generic string. Only the section's progress reads and names the reason code, and tests hold this.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the execution of T44: the task has no planning, and R29 proposed `#/adults/progress` from the profile page for it; the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 the tasks T36–T48 ran in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision (RUN.md, T44, «Сделано»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.8 and 3.10; the normative text is [the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "Progress".
- **Where.** Progress belongs to a profile, and by R29 it is a part of the hash and a button on the profile page: the section "Progress" under "Modules", a line saying what is on the page, and "See the progress". The profile is the one chosen in the section; without it — after a reload — both the progress and a module's map lead to the section's first page in their own place in the history, as the profile page does. The language is the device's, as for the whole section (R10), and the heading is "Progress: {pseudonym}".
- **Skill maps.** The section "Skill maps" shows the connected modules of `ListConnectedModules` as the cards of "My subjects"; a module with a current version has "Open the map", a module without one has "Not available now" and no button. A module's map has the heading "Skill map: {module}", "Back to the progress" and the element with `view="adult"`, the device's language and the bands' headings as `h2`, without `skillchoose`: the adult does not choose a skill (R03 and R04 skill-map). The data is `readModuleMap` of T43 with `ACTION_ADULT`, the right `can_view` (R34). A module without a graph has "This subject has no skill map".
- **Repeating traps** come only from the first page, the one requested with an empty `page_token`; the traps of other pages, if `api` sends them, are not shown. Each has its `description` as a heading and its `trap_advice` as "Advice", both with the `lang` of the module's explanation language; the module; "Found in {N} closed attempts" and "Last time". These are the only texts of a trap that reach the device ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 16), and only here.
- **Attempts** come in the order of the response, the newest first. "Show more" requests the next page by `next_page_token` and puts its attempts below, and the focus on the heading of the first of them (WCAG 2.4.3), and for a page without attempts on the heading of the last one shown or, if there are no attempts at all, on the heading "Attempts"; the last page has no button. A failure of a next page puts its words above the button, and the same button requests the same page; a failure of the first puts its words and "Try again" under the heading "Attempts": the first page carries both the traps and the attempts, and a failure under "Skill maps" would read as theirs. Each attempt has the heading "Task {ID}" and the terms:
  - "Module" — the name from the manifest of the connected module's current version, otherwise from the newest manifest the cache holds, otherwise the ID, with the `lang` of its explanation language. The cards of the connected modules are the same as those of "Skill maps"; the cache is read only when an attempt or a trap names a module that is not among them or whose name did not come, and by the same `cardsOf` as the cards, so the rule is one;
  - "Lesson" — for an attempt of a segment; "Version";
  - "Outcome" — by the close reason and the wrong answers: "Still going", "Answered correctly at the first answer" — `CLOSE_REASON_CORRECT` without wrong answers —, "Answered correctly", "Solution shown", "Left: another task was begun", "Nothing was done on it for 7 days", "Not checked: this version of the module is no longer given", "Ended: its lesson doesn't suit this profile any more", "Finished with Done"; an unfamiliar reason gets the generic string;
  - "Wrong answers" — except for an essay with submissions; "Begun" and "Closed" — the date and time in the screen's language, `<time>`;
  - "Why it was given", and for an attempt of a segment "Why its lesson was given": the words of the reason code and "Chosen". Without `chosen_at`, "The learner began it again: the program chose nothing": such an attempt has no code ([the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15).

  A place for an essay's submissions — with their end and the criterion marks (R87 spec) — is left under the attempt's terms: T47 builds them.
- **The words of the codes** — nine, by concepts 12 spec, R100 and R107 spec — are "Consolidation: after a wrong answer, another task on the same skill"; "A new skill: one not given before, whose prerequisites are counted as met, or which has none"; "Next in the queue: of the skills within reach given before and not mastered, the one not given for longest"; "Review: the time to review a mastered skill has come"; "A probe: a skill put aside comes back as a check"; "The learner chose the skill on the skill map, and the program the task or lesson on it"; "The author's order: a skill with a rubric or with no assessment comes next by the skill graph"; "The lesson begun: the learner goes on with it"; "In rotation: a skill given before comes round again, the one not given for longest first". `REASON_CODE_OPEN_RUN` never occurs in the progress — an attempt of a segment has the code of the choice that began the lesson run — but it has words: the type requires words for every value. The engine in the words is "the program": so the adult understands that a rule makes the choice, not a person and not the AI (R03 spec). A code the build does not know, and a code with no value beside `chosen_at`, get the generic string "This version of the app doesn't know this yet": it holds no reason, as R100 spec requires, and section 3.8 gives it to any unfamiliar code.
- **The code is the adult's alone** (concepts 12 spec; R100 spec): only `src/adults/ProgressPage.tsx` calls `GetProgress`, through `readProgress`, and only it and `src/adults/progress.ts` know the field `reason_code` and the words of the codes. A test in Node walks the sources of `src/` and holds this, and a test in Chromium goes through the learner's screens — "My subjects", "Something else", the task and the skill map — in the profile's language and finds none of the eighteen texts of the codes and no `GetProgress`, and in the section finds all nine.
- **The shared module card** is `src/subjects/ModuleCards.tsx`: `ModuleCardItem` is a card with one button, described by the name, `ModuleCardList` is the order by name, the mark "In order" and "Try again" for names that did not come, and `useConnectedCards` is the profile's connected modules. They now build the list of skill maps (R34), the profile's modules (R32) and the progress; "My subjects" keeps its own cards — two lists and the focus that leaves with a card. This closes the finding of the review of T43 about a third copy.

— *Why:*
- a module's map is a page of its own: the element draws one map, and there are as many maps as modules, and all of them at once on one page would be several screens long; the address holds which map is open, and the browser's "Back" leads to the progress;
- the traps are above the attempts: they are few and come from the first page, while the attempts grow downward with the button "Show more";
- "Show more", and not page numbers or loading on scroll: a page by a token gives only the next one, and a button is reachable from the keyboard and does not move the page by itself;
- the local IDs of the task and the lesson: the progress names them only by full IDs, a task has no name in the format, and `app` gets the open view of a lesson only from `OpenLesson` — a learning action that begins a lesson run. This is В57 «в реестр spec»;
- the generic string for an unfamiliar code is the same as for any unfamiliar code (section 3.8), and it holds no reason that the code may not mean.

— *Rejected:*
- all the maps on the progress page, one after another, and one map with the module chosen by buttons on the same page — the argument about length above, and a choice without an address would not hold "Back";
- reading all the pages of `GetProgress` at once — a request for every hundred attempts, while the adult may be looking only at the last ones;
- the lesson's name through `OpenLesson` — a learning request with the right `can_learn`, which begins a lesson run for the learner; the task statement instead of a name — before the solution, the open view of a task comes only with the start of an attempt;
- a phrase of its own for an unfamiliar code, "for a reason this version does not name" — section 3.8 has already decided the generic string, and two strings for one case would drift apart;
- the reason code beside "What's next" for the learner — concepts 12 spec and R100 spec forbid it, and the contract does not give it there anyway.

— *Does not decide:* the rubric marks of essays — T47, in the place left; the names of tasks and lessons in the progress — В57; a line telling the child that the adult sees their progress — E4 (Д157 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`); filtering the attempts by module or time — the contract does not give it.

— *Consequence:* `src/adults/progress.ts` — the routes, `readProgress`, `entryOf`, `outcomeKey`, `CHOICE_REASON_KEYS` and `choiceReasonKey`; `src/adults/ProgressPage.tsx` — `ProgressPage` and `ProgressMap`; the pages `progress` and `progress/<module>` in `src/adults/AdultSection.tsx`; the section "Progress" in `src/adults/ProfilePage.tsx`; `src/subjects/ModuleCards.tsx`, with `src/adults/ModulesPage.tsx` and `src/skills/SkillMapScreen.tsx` on it; `mapTitle` and `MapHeading` — in `src/skills/SkillMap.tsx`; the keys `progress.*` in the dictionaries. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.8 and 3.10; RUN.md, T44 and В57.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12:* "Lesson" is the lesson's name `lesson_title`, with the `lang` of the module's explanation language, and the local ID only when there is no name; a solved attempt also has "Skills", the names of the skills of its task, after "Lesson" (R40). В57 is closed by the 2026-11-12 amendment to R99 spec, and "Does not decide" about the names no longer holds; the format still has no names of tasks, and a task is named by its local ID.

**R36. Voicing in code is the module `src/voice/`: one synthesis for the app, the voice by R07 and section 3.5, and the platform by `navigator.userAgentData.platform`, without it by the user agent string. The "Slow" speed is `rate` 0.75 until T51 finds the number on devices (Д5). The permission of voicing on Android and Linux and the voice chosen for a language are the record `voicing` of the device registry. The learner turns voicing on and off with the button "Voicing" at the top of the task screen — `SetVoicing` — and the session takes the profile from the response. The check's verdict and the neutral phrase, the rung's words, the correct answer words, "Explain differently" and the hint sound by themselves, and "Read aloud" stands at the task statement, the tutor's words, the hint, the step, the explanation and the worked example. Signs sound as words by the table for `en` and `ru` (Д6). Where a text will not sound, the button is inactive, and a line beside it describes it and does not serve as a status.** The executor's decision of 2026-11-12 in the planning of T45: the author was unavailable, and by the author's one-time instruction of 2026-11-12 the tasks T36–T48 ran in a row without the author; the author reviews the decision and proofreads the words `voice.*` in `en` and `ru` and the table of signs (RUN.md, T45, «Решено при планировании»). The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5, "Code", and sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.8 and 3.9; the source of the speed's number is section 4, Web Speech API.
- **Speed.** "Normal" is `rate` 1: the specification calls it the voice's default rate, "which should correspond to a normal speaking rate". "Slow" is 0.75, a quarter slower. `VOICING_SPEED_UNSPECIFIED` and a value the build does not know are "normal". The number is not checked: each engine reads `rate` its own way, and the specification speaks of it only relatively (section 4). T51 finds it — Д5 in table 4 of RUN.md.
- **Platform.** The values "Android", "Chrome OS" and "Linux" of `userAgentData.platform` are these platforms. "Windows", "macOS" and "iOS" wait for no permission. "Fuchsia", "Unknown", an empty value and a browser without `userAgentData` go by the user agent string: "Android" before "CrOS", "CrOS" before "Linux" or "X11". iPadOS calls itself Mac, and there is no need to tell it apart: an empty utterance goes with each tap after which the tutor's words will come — "Check", "Hint", "Explain differently" — on all platforms, while voicing is on and permitted, and "Read aloud" speaks by itself within the tap.
- **The device's record.** The key `voicing` of the registry is `{allowed, voices}`, where `voices` is a `voiceURI` per language tag in lower case. Reading does not create the database, and a record that cannot be read means "nothing chosen and no permission". The owner's sign-out erases it with the registry, and a sign-in of another account — `accountSeen` and `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS` — erases the record itself: the other account decides for itself (R07; section 3.2). A failure to write gives the line "The choice wasn't kept on this device", and what is shown stays as before. The page "This device" announces the record to other tabs by `BroadcastChannel`, and the learner's screens there read it again: a permission taken back holds at once. Until the record is read, nothing sounds, and the buttons are inactive without a line — the line would only flash.
- **The learner's button** is "Voicing", `aria-pressed`, at the top of the task screen, above the work. This refines the words of section 3.5 "with a button on the tutor's panel": in the mode "self" there is no panel before the solution, and the learner must turn voicing on before the words come. `SetVoicing` sends only `enabled`, and the speed stays as it was. The session takes the profile from the response as the profile page's save does, and tells other tabs about it (R29). While the request is under way, the button is `aria-disabled`; a failure gives its words, `role="alert"`. Turning it off stops speech at once — by this button and by any other path: the profile page, another tab, a permission taken back. If voicing is on and the language does not sound, the line under the button says why.
- **What sounds by itself** — only when voicing is on, and on Android and Linux also permitted:
  - after "Check" — "Correct" or "Not right yet: …" and the neutral phrase of the AI call outcome, in the screen's language; after them, without cutting them off, the rung's words or the correct answer words, in the explanation language;
  - on "Hint" — what it gave: the givens, the unknown, the cards of techniques and the question, or the rung's words;
  - on "Explain differently" — the new words.

  Not sounding by themselves:
  - the lines at items — what was read and why it cannot be read: they stand at the item, not on the tutor's panel, and the screen reader reads them;
  - the task's solution;
  - the words with which the attempt opened after a reload: no tap called them.
- **"Read aloud"** is at the task statement above the work, at the tutor's words, at the hint, at the step, at the lesson's explanation and at the worked example. What sounds is listed by "Code" of section 3.5. In an example, a box the working has already filled sounds as its value, and a hidden working does not sound at all, with the boxes of its lines. A second tap while its text sounds stops it, a tap on another button cuts the earlier text off, and a button that leaves the screen — with a lesson's segment, with a hint — stops its own. A task screen that leaves stops what it said itself, and the words that came after it do not sound at all. The button sounds also with voicing off, and on Android and Linux only after the permission. The icon is `volume-up`, without fill, as it is from right to left: this is a value of `app`'s own, `read-aloud` (R78 spec; section 3.1).
- **The line "will not sound".** The button is not `disabled` but `aria-disabled`, so as not to lose focus. The line beside it — "This device has no voice for …" or "On this device, an adult turns voicing on first" — is the button's description, `aria-describedby`, not `role="status"`. This refines the row 4.1.3 of section 3.9.
- **Signs in words (Д6)** are a table for `en` and `ru` in "Code" of section 3.5, by the first subtag of the explanation language:
  - a sign is read as an action when there is an operand beside it on either side — a digit, a box or a bracket — and without an operand the sign is named;
  - a hyphen between numbers with spaces is a minus;
